Write: elements/snowpeak/src/snowmodel.py
"""snowmodel.py -- where the snow lies on a real mountain, for a season.

The observation half of the study, as a program a simulation can port.  Every step is a published
process with its numbers (sources in LESSONS.md, part 1):

 1. accumulation   HS_acc(z) = g_acc * (z - z_rs), clipped to [0, hs_max]      (orographic gradient)
 2. wind           HS *= clip(1 + k_wind * Sx, lo, hi)  (Winstral 2002 Sx: upwind max slope angle;
                   Sx < 0 exposed crest -> scour, Sx > 0 sheltered lee -> loading)
 3. gravity        each cell holds at most SHD(slope) = c * exp(-a * slope) m  (Bernhardt & Schulz 2010:
                   3.08, 1.11, 0.45, 0.15 m at 30, 45, 60, 75 deg); the excess slides to lower
                   neighbours (multiple flow direction), processed from the top down, so steep faces
                   shed and gullies, ledges, bowls and slope-foot cones collect.
 4. melt           melt(z, I) = HS_acc(z_sl) * (1 + k_melt * (z_sl - z) / 1000) * (I / I_flat) ** p_ins
                   z_sl is the season's snowline for undisturbed, flat, average snow; I is the
                   clear-sky insolation over the melt season, including terrain shadows.
 5. glaciers       WorldCover snow/ice cells: where seasonal snow survives, firn (white); where it has
                   gone, bare glacier ice (grey-blue) with crevasse fields on steep ice.

    S = snow(T, SEASONS["sept"], wind_from=225)
    S["hs"]      remaining snow depth, m
    S["cover"]   0..1 cover fraction (sub-grid: ledges on steep rock hold a streaky fraction)
    S["glacier"] 0 none, 1 firn-covered glacier, 2 bare ice
    S["cornice"] 0..1 cornice lip strength on lee crest cells
"""
import numpy as np
from numba import njit
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
import sun as sunmod

SEASONS = {
    # z_sl: snowline elevation (m) of undisturbed flat snow;  depth parameters for the winter
    "spring": dict(z_sl=1250, label="late May"),
    "summer": dict(z_sl=1700, label="mid July"),
    "sept": dict(z_sl=2150, label="September minimum"),
    "fresh": dict(z_sl=2150, fresh=0.15, fresh_z=1600, label="September, after first autumn snow"),
    "winter": dict(z_sl=300, label="midwinter"),
}
DEFAULT = dict(z_rs=300.0, g_acc=0.45 / 100, hs_max=9.0,          # 0.45 m depth per 100 m; cap 9 m
               k_wind=0.035, wind_lo=0.15, wind_hi=2.5, sx_dmax=300.0,
               shd_c=23.0, shd_a=0.067, shd_min=0.03,              # fit to Bernhardt & Schulz defaults
               k_melt=0.9, p_ins=0.8, doy_melt=(135, 250))


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- terrain fields
def fields(Z, res):
    gy, gx = np.gradient(Z.astype(np.float64), res)
    gy = -gy                                                      # row 0 = north: dZ/dN = -dZ/drow
    slope = np.degrees(np.arctan(np.hypot(gx, gy)))
    aspect = (np.degrees(np.arctan2(-gx, -gy)) + 360) % 360       # downslope direction, deg from N
    n = np.dstack([-gx, -gy, np.ones_like(gx)]); n /= np.linalg.norm(n, axis=2, keepdims=True)
    return slope, aspect, n


def curvature(Z, res, r_m):
    """height minus local mean over radius r_m: <0 hollow/gully, >0 crest/rib"""
    s = max(r_m / res / 2, 0.5)
    return Z - ndi.gaussian_filter(Z.astype(np.float64), s)


@njit(cache=True)
def _sx(Z, res, dx, dy, dmax):
    ny, nx = Z.shape
    out = np.zeros((ny, nx))
    nstep = int(dmax / res)
    for i in range(ny):
        for j in range(nx):
            z0 = Z[i, j]; best = -1e9
            for k in range(1, nstep + 1):
                ii = int(round(i - dy * k)); jj = int(round(j + dx * k))    # step upwind (dy north = -row)
                if ii < 0 or ii >= ny or jj < 0 or jj >= nx:
                    break
                a = np.arctan((Z[ii, jj] - z0) / (k * res))
                if a > best:
                    best = a
            out[i, j] = np.degrees(best) if best > -1e8 else 0.0
    return out


def winstral_sx(Z, res, wind_from=225.0, dmax=300.0, spread=30.0):
    """Sx averaged over wind_from +- spread (Winstral et al. 2002)"""
    acc = np.zeros(Z.shape)
    angs = [wind_from - spread, wind_from, wind_from + spread]
    for a in angs:
        r = np.radians(a)
        acc += _sx(Z.astype(np.float64), res, np.sin(r), np.cos(r), dmax)
    return acc / len(angs)


@njit(cache=True)
def _shadow(Z, res, sx, sy, sz, maxd):
    """1 = sunlit, 0 = in terrain shadow, for sun direction (sx east, sy north, sz up)"""
    ny, nx = Z.shape
    out = np.ones((ny, nx), np.float32)
    h = np.sqrt(sx * sx + sy * sy) + 1e-9
    tan_e = sz / h
    ux = sx / h; uy = sy / h
    nstep = int(maxd / res)
    for i in range(ny):
        for j in range(nx):
            z0 = Z[i, j] + 1.0
            for k in range(1, nstep):
                d = k * res * (1.0 + k * 0.02)          # stretch steps with distance
                ii = int(i - uy * d / res); jj = int(j + ux * d / res)
                if ii < 0 or ii >= ny or jj < 0 or jj >= nx:
                    break
                if Z[ii, jj] > z0 + d * tan_e:
                    out[i, j] = 0.0
                    break
                if z0 + d * tan_e > 5000:
                    break
    return out


def shadow(Z, res, az, el, maxd=15000.0):
    v = sunmod.vec(az, el)
    return _shadow(Z.astype(np.float64), res, v[0], v[1], v[2], maxd)


def insolation(Z, res, n, lat, lon, doys=(172, 200, 230), hours=range(6, 20), coarse=2):
    """relative clear-sky direct + diffuse energy over the melt season, with cast shadows.
    Returned as ratio to a flat unshadowed cell (1.0 = flat)."""
    Zc = Z[::coarse, ::coarse]; nc = n[::coarse, ::coarse]
    tot = np.zeros(Zc.shape); flat = 0.0
    from datetime import datetime, timezone, timedelta
    for d in doys:
        for h in hours:
            t = datetime(2021, 1, 1, tzinfo=timezone.utc) + timedelta(days=d - 1, hours=h + 7)
            az, el = sunmod.position(lat, lon, t)
            if el < 2:
                continue
            v = sunmod.vec(az, el)
            m = 1.0 / max(np.sin(np.radians(el)), 0.05)              # air mass
            beam = 0.75 ** (m ** 0.678)                                # clear-sky transmittance
            cosi = np.clip(nc @ v, 0, None)
            sh = shadow(Zc, res * coarse, az, el, maxd=8000.0)
            tot += beam * cosi * sh + 0.12 * (1 + nc[..., 2]) / 2
            flat += beam * v[2] + 0.12
    I = tot / flat
    return ndi.zoom(I, coarse, order=1)[:Z.shape[0], :Z.shape[1]]


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- gravity
@njit(cache=True)
def _route(Z, hs, shd, res):
    ny, nx = Z.shape
    order = np.argsort(-Z.ravel())
    out = hs.copy()
    di = np.array([-1, -1, -1, 0, 0, 1, 1, 1]); dj = np.array([-1, 0, 1, -1, 1, -1, 0, 1])
    dist = np.array([1.414, 1.0, 1.414, 1.0, 1.0, 1.414, 1.0, 1.414]) * res
    w = np.zeros(8)
    for idx in order:
        i = idx // nx; j = idx % nx
        ex = out[i, j] - shd[i, j]
        if ex <= 0:
            continue
        tw = 0.0
        for k in range(8):
            ii = i + di[k]; jj = j + dj[k]
            w[k] = 0.0
            if 0 <= ii < ny and 0 <= jj < nx:
                dz = Z[i, j] - Z[ii, jj]
                if dz > 0:
                    w[k] = (dz / dist[k]) ** 1.1
                    tw += w[k]
        if tw <= 0:
            continue                                   # pit: keep it (snow fills the hollow)
        out[i, j] = shd[i, j]
        for k in range(8):
            if w[k] > 0:
                out[i + di[k], j + dj[k]] += ex * w[k] / tw
    return out


def holding_depth(slope, P=DEFAULT):
    return np.maximum(P["shd_c"] * np.exp(-P["shd_a"] * slope), P["shd_min"])


# ---------------------------------------------------------------- the model
def snow(T, season, wind_from=225.0, P=None, lat=47.0, lon=-121.5, cache=None, want_I=True):
    P = dict(DEFAULT, **(P or {}))
    Z = T["Z"].astype(np.float64); res = T["res"]
    slope, aspect, n = fields(Z, res)
    hs = np.clip(P["g_acc"] * (Z - P["z_rs"]), 0, P["hs_max"])
    sx = winstral_sx(Z, res, wind_from, P["sx_dmax"])
    hs_w = hs * np.clip(1 + P["k_wind"] * sx, P["wind_lo"], P["wind_hi"])
    shd = holding_depth(slope, P)
    hs_g = _route(Z, hs_w, shd, res)
    if want_I:
        I = insolation(Z, res, n, lat, lon)
    else:
        I = np.clip(0.9 + 0.5 * (n[..., 1] * -1) * 0 + 0.6 * n @ sunmod.vec(180, 55), 0.1, 2)
    z_sl = season["z_sl"]
    acc_sl = np.clip(P["g_acc"] * (z_sl - P["z_rs"]), 0, P["hs_max"])
    melt = acc_sl * np.clip(1 + P["k_melt"] * (z_sl - Z) / 1000, 0.05, None) * np.clip(I, 0.05, None) ** P["p_ins"]
    rem = hs_g - melt
    # a thin fresh autumn snowfall above fresh_z (lies everywhere it can hold, sheds from steep rock)
    if season.get("fresh"):
        fr = season["fresh"] * np.clip((Z - season["fresh_z"]) / 300, 0, 1)
        rem = np.maximum(rem, 0) + np.minimum(fr, shd)
    hs_rem = np.maximum(rem, 0)
    ice = T.get("ice", np.zeros_like(Z))
    glacier = np.where(ice > 0.5, np.where(hs_rem > 0.05, 1, 2), 0).astype(np.int8)
    # sub-grid cover: deep snow covers fully; thin snow on steep rock survives on ledges only
    cover = np.clip(hs_rem / 0.4, 0, 1)
    steep = np.clip((slope - 40) / 25, 0, 1)
    cover = cover * (1 - 0.55 * steep)                 # a 65-deg face shows ~45% ledge snow at best
    cover = np.where(glacier > 0, 1.0, cover)
    # cornices: lee crest cells (sheltering drop downwind) under an exposed windward approach
    crest = curvature(Z, res, 60) > 2.0 * res / 10
    r = np.radians(wind_from)
    down = ndi.shift(Z, (np.cos(r) * 2, np.sin(r) * 2), order=1, mode="nearest")   # 2 cells downwind
    drop = (Z - down) / (2 * res)
    cornice = crest & (sx < -3) & (drop > np.tan(np.radians(35))) & (hs_w > 1.0)
    return dict(hs=hs_rem, hs_acc=hs, hs_wind=hs_w, hs_grav=hs_g, cover=cover, glacier=glacier,
                cornice=cornice.astype(np.float32), sx=sx, I=I, slope=slope, aspect=aspect, n=n,
                shd=shd, melt=melt)

Write: elements/snowpeak/src/scene.py
"""scene.py -- one view of a real peak: DEM, snow model, camera, per-pixel G-buffer.

    S = Scene("rainier_ne_july", W=320)        # uses cache/cams.json + fitcam.SHOTS
    S.snow("summer")                            # runs snowmodel on the peak sub-box
    G = S.gbuffer()                             # per-pixel fields for the painter
"""
import numpy as np, json, os
from scipy.ndimage import map_coordinates
import dem, cam, fitcam, snowmodel, sun as sunmod

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))


class Scene:
    def __init__(self, shot, W=320, H=None, cams=None, snow_half_km=11, snow_res=15, cam_override=None):
        self.shot = shot
        s = fitcam.SHOTS[shot]; self.s = s
        c = (cams or json.load(open(os.path.join(HERE, "../cache/cams.json"))))[shot]
        if cam_override:
            c = dict(c, **cam_override)
        ce, cn, pe, pn, bx = fitcam.box(s)
        self.T = dem.load_box(*[round(v) for v in bx], s["res"], tag=shot)
        from PIL import Image
        pw, ph = Image.open(s["photo"]).size
        self.W = W; self.H = H or round(W * ph / pw)
        self.C = cam.Camera(c["e"], c["n"], c["z"], c["az"], c["pitch"], c["hfov"], self.W, self.H, c["roll"])
        lat, lon, _ = dem.PEAKS[s["peak"]]
        self.lat, self.lon = lat, lon
        self.Tp = dem.load(s["peak"], snow_half_km, snow_res)      # snow sub-box around the peak
        y, mo, d, h, mi = s["time"]
        self.sun = sunmod.local(lat, lon, y, mo, d, h, mi)

    def snow(self, season, wind_from=225.0, P=None):
        key = f"{self.s['peak']}_{season}_{int(wind_from)}_{hash(str(sorted((P or {}).items()))) % 10000}"
        fn = os.path.join(HERE, f"../cache/snow_{key}.npz")
        if os.path.exists(fn):
            self.S = dict(np.load(fn))
        else:
            S = snowmodel.snow(self.Tp, snowmodel.SEASONS[season], wind_from, P, self.lat, self.lon)
            keep = {k: S[k].astype(np.float16 if S[k].dtype.kind == "f" else S[k].dtype)
                    for k in ("hs", "cover", "glacier", "cornice", "sx", "I", "hs_grav", "hs_acc")}
            np.savez_compressed(fn, **keep)
            self.S = keep
        self.season = season
        return self.S

    def gbuffer(self, sun=None):
        G = cam.cast(self.T, self.C)
        T = self.T; Tp = self.Tp
        sl, asp, n = snowmodel.fields(T["Z"], T["res"])
        def samp(A, r, c, order=1):
            return map_coordinates(np.asarray(A, np.float32), [r.ravel(), c.ravel()], order=order,
                                   mode="nearest").reshape(r.shape)
        r, c = G["r"], G["c"]
        G["normal"] = np.stack([samp(n[..., k], r, c) for k in range(3)], -1)
        G["normal"] /= np.linalg.norm(G["normal"], axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        G["slope"] = samp(sl, r, c)
        for k in ("ice", "trees", "bare"):
            G[k] = samp(T[k], r, c)
        # snow fields from the peak sub-box (outside it: none, the sub-box is chosen to hold all snow)
        rp = (Tp["y0"] - G["n"]) / Tp["res"] - 0.5; cp = (G["e"] - Tp["x0"]) / Tp["res"] - 0.5
        inside = (rp > 0) & (rp < Tp["Z"].shape[0] - 1) & (cp > 0) & (cp < Tp["Z"].shape[1] - 1) & ~G["sky"]
        for k in ("hs", "cover", "cornice", "I"):
            G[k] = np.where(inside, samp(self.S[k], rp, cp), 0.0)
        G["glacier"] = np.where(inside, samp(self.S["glacier"], rp, cp, order=0), 0)
        # sun shadow at each hit point, by marching the peak box (fine) then the view box
        az, el = sun or self.sun
        self.sun_used = (az, el)
        shp = snowmodel.shadow(Tp["Z"], Tp["res"], az, el, maxd=20000.0) if el > -3 else np.zeros_like(Tp["Z"])
        shv = snowmodel.shadow(T["Z"][::2, ::2], T["res"] * 2, az, el, maxd=30000.0) if el > -3 else None
        sh_view = samp(shv, r / 2, c / 2) if shv is not None else np.zeros(r.shape)
        G["sunlit"] = np.where(inside, samp(shp, rp, cp), sh_view)
        G["sunvec"] = sunmod.vec(az, max(el, 0.5))
        G["ndl"] = G["normal"] @ G["sunvec"]
        # sub-pixel curvature for gully/rib reading
        cv = snowmodel.curvature(T["Z"], T["res"], 90)
        G["curv"] = samp(cv, r, c)
        self.G = G
        return G

"""quick physical preview of a scene: where snow falls, before any painting."""
import numpy as np, sys, time
from PIL import Image
import scene
def preview(S, G):
    cov=np.clip(G["cover"],0,1); gl=G["glacier"]
    alb_rock=np.where(G["trees"]>0.5,0.05,np.where(G["bare"]>0.3,0.2,0.12))
    alb=alb_rock*(1-cov)+0.85*cov
    alb=np.where(gl==2,0.45,alb)
    light=np.clip(G["ndl"],0,1)*G["sunlit"]*1.0+0.28*(0.5+0.5*G["normal"][...,2])
    v=alb*light
    col=np.stack([v*1.0,v*1.02,v*1.08],-1)
    d=np.where(G["sky"],1e9,G["dist"]); f=1-np.exp(-d/60000)
    sky=np.array([0.55,0.68,0.9])
    col=col*(1-f[...,None])+sky*0.9*f[...,None]
    col[G["sky"]]=sky
    return (np.clip(col,0,1)**(1/2.2)*255).astype(np.uint8)
if __name__=="__main__":
    shot=sys.argv[1]; season=sys.argv[2]; W=int(sys.argv[3]) if len(sys.argv)>3 else 480
    t=time.time(); S=scene.Scene(shot,W=W); S.snow(season); print("snow",time.time()-t)
    t=time.time(); G=S.gbuffer(); print("gbuf",time.time()-t, "sun",S.sun)
    im=preview(S,G)
    ph=Image.open(S.s["photo"]).convert("RGB").resize((S.W,S.H),Image.LANCZOS)
    out=Image.new("RGB",(S.W*2,S.H)); out.paste(ph,(0,0)); out.paste(Image.fromarray(im),(S.W,0))
    out.save(f"../stages/preview_{shot}_{season}.png")
EOF

Bash: Compare modeled vs photo snow cover
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; cat > snowcmp.py <<'EOF'
"""compare the model's snow cover with the photo's snow (L* threshold) inside the terrain area."""
import numpy as np, sys
from PIL import Image
from skimage.color import rgb2lab
import scene
def photo_snow(path,W,H,thr=60):
    lab=rgb2lab(np.asarray(Image.open(path).convert("RGB").resize((W,H),Image.LANCZOS))/255.)
    return lab[...,0]>thr, lab
def compare(S,G,thr=60,out=None):
    ps,lab=photo_snow(S.s["photo"],S.W,S.H,thr)
    ms=(G["cover"]>0.5)|(G["glacier"]>0)
    roi=~G["sky"]&(G["z"]>1500)
    inter=(ps&ms&roi).sum(); uni=((ps|ms)&roi).sum()
    iou=inter/max(uni,1)
    img=np.zeros((S.H,S.W,3),np.uint8)
    img[roi]=(60,60,60); img[ps&ms&roi]=(240,240,240); img[ps&~ms&roi]=(40,120,255); img[~ps&ms&roi]=(255,90,40)
    if out: Image.fromarray(img).resize((S.W*2,S.H*2),Image.NEAREST).save(out)
    return iou,(ps&roi).mean(),(ms&roi).mean()
if __name__=="__main__":
    shot,season=sys.argv[1],sys.argv[2]
    S=scene.Scene(shot,W=480); S.snow(season); G=S.gbuffer()
    print(compare(S,G,out=f"../stages/snowcmp_{shot}_{season}.png"))
EOF

Bash: Split snow model into prepare/apply with caching
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("snowmodel.py").read()
a=s.index("def snow(T, season")
s=s[:a]+'''def prepare(T, wind_from=225.0, P=None, lat=47.0, lon=-121.5):
    """the season-independent, expensive part: fields, wind, gravity, insolation"""
    P = dict(DEFAULT, **(P or {}))
    Z = T["Z"].astype(np.float64); res = T["res"]
    slope, aspect, n = fields(Z, res)
    hs = np.clip(P["g_acc"] * (Z - P["z_rs"]), 0, P["hs_max"])
    sx = winstral_sx(Z, res, wind_from, P["sx_dmax"])
    hs_w = hs * np.clip(1 + P["k_wind"] * sx, P["wind_lo"], P["wind_hi"])
    shd = holding_depth(slope, P)
    hs_g = _route(Z, hs_w, shd, res)
    I = insolation(Z, res, n, lat, lon)
    crest = curvature(Z, res, 60) > 2.0 * res / 10
    r = np.radians(wind_from)
    down = ndi.shift(Z, (np.cos(r) * 2, -np.sin(r) * 2), order=1, mode="nearest")   # 2 cells downwind
    drop = (Z - down) / (2 * res)
    cornice = crest & (sx < -3) & (drop > np.tan(np.radians(35))) & (hs_w > 1.0)
    return dict(Z=Z, slope=slope, aspect=aspect, hs_acc=hs, hs_wind=hs_w, hs_grav=hs_g, sx=sx, I=I,
                shd=shd, cornice=cornice.astype(np.float32), ice=np.asarray(T.get("ice", np.zeros_like(Z))),
                P=P)


def apply(prep, season, P=None):
    """the cheap, seasonal part: melt, fresh snow, glacier surface state, sub-grid cover"""
    Pd = dict(prep["P"], **(P or {}))
    Z, slope, I, shd = prep["Z"], prep["slope"], prep["I"], prep["shd"]
    z_sl = season["z_sl"]
    acc_sl = np.clip(Pd["g_acc"] * (z_sl - Pd["z_rs"]), 0, Pd["hs_max"])
    melt = acc_sl * np.clip(1 + Pd["k_melt"] * (z_sl - Z) / 1000, 0.05, None) * np.clip(I, 0.05, None) ** Pd["p_ins"]
    rem = prep["hs_grav"] - melt
    if season.get("fresh"):
        fr = season["fresh"] * np.clip((Z - season["fresh_z"]) / 300, 0, 1)
        rem = np.maximum(rem, 0) + np.minimum(fr, shd)
    hs_rem = np.maximum(rem, 0)
    ice = prep["ice"]
    # glacier ice lies under the mask, but faces steeper than ~50 deg inside it are rock walls (Willis Wall)
    on_ice = (ice > 0.5) & (slope < Pd.get("ice_max_slope", 50))
    glacier = np.where(on_ice, np.where(hs_rem > 0.05, 1, 2), 0).astype(np.int8)
    cover = np.clip(hs_rem / 0.4, 0, 1)
    steep = np.clip((slope - 40) / 25, 0, 1)
    cover = cover * (1 - 0.55 * steep)
    cover = np.where(glacier > 0, 1.0, cover)
    return dict(hs=hs_rem, cover=cover, glacier=glacier, cornice=prep["cornice"], sx=prep["sx"], I=I,
                hs_grav=prep["hs_grav"], hs_acc=prep["hs_acc"], melt=melt)


def snow(T, season, wind_from=225.0, P=None, lat=47.0, lon=-121.5):
    return apply(prepare(T, wind_from, P, lat, lon), season, P)
'''
open("snowmodel.py","w").write(s)

        self.S = snowmodel.apply(prep, se, P)

        return self.S

Bash: Measure photo Lab values by class at 21 and 96 km
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; cat > measure.py <<'EOF'
"""measure.py shot [season] : photo Lab by class (snow/rock x lit/shade, forest, sky), using the
fitted camera's G-buffer to know what each photo pixel looks at and how far away it is."""
import numpy as np, sys, json
from PIL import Image
from skimage.color import rgb2lab
import scene
def measure(S,G,snow_thr=None):
    lab=rgb2lab(np.asarray(Image.open(S.s["photo"]).convert("RGB").resize((S.W,S.H),Image.LANCZOS))/255.)
    L=lab[...,0]; terr=~G["sky"]
    # photo snow: Otsu-like split of L* over the terrain above 1600 m
    hi=terr&(G["z"]>1600)
    from skimage.filters import threshold_otsu
    thr=snow_thr or threshold_otsu(L[hi])
    psnow=hi&(L>thr)
    lit=(G["ndl"]>0.15)&(G["sunlit"]>0.5); shade=(G["ndl"]<0.0)|(G["sunlit"]<0.5)
    cls=dict(snow_lit=psnow&lit, snow_shade=psnow&shade,
             rock_lit=hi&~psnow&lit&(G["trees"]<0.3), rock_shade=hi&~psnow&shade&(G["trees"]<0.3),
             forest=terr&(G["trees"]>0.6)&(G["z"]<1500))
    # sky: 20 rows just above the terrain silhouette
    sk=G["sky"]&np.roll(terr,-15,0); cls["sky_low"]=sk
    cls["sky_top"]=np.zeros_like(sk); cls["sky_top"][:max(3,S.H//12)]=True
    out={"thr":float(thr)}
    for k,m in cls.items():
        if m.sum()<8: continue
        d=G["dist"][m]; d=d[np.isfinite(d)]
        out[k]=dict(n=int(m.sum()),L=round(float(np.median(L[m])),1),a=round(float(np.median(lab[...,1][m])),1),
                    b=round(float(np.median(lab[...,2][m])),1),dist_km=round(float(np.median(d))/1000,1) if len(d) else None)
    return out
if __name__=="__main__":
    shot=sys.argv[1]; season=sys.argv[2] if len(sys.argv)>2 else "summer"
    S=scene.Scene(shot,W=480); S.snow(season); G=S.gbuffer()
    m=measure(S,G); print(json.dumps(m,indent=0))
    db=json.load(open("../cache/measure.json")) if __import__("os").path.exists("../cache/measure.json") else {}
    db[shot]=m; json.dump(db,open("../cache/measure.json","w"),indent=1)
EOF

"""boxes.py: named-box Lab samples from a photo (the observation student's point samples).
Coordinates in original photo pixels.  Prints median L*a*b* and the 10/90 percentiles of L*."""
import numpy as np, json, sys
from PIL import Image
from skimage.color import rgb2lab
def sample(path, boxes):
    lab = rgb2lab(np.asarray(Image.open(path).convert("RGB")) / 255.0)
    out = {}
    for k, (x0, y0, x1, y1) in boxes.items():
        a = lab[y0:y1, x0:x1].reshape(-1, 3)
        out[k] = dict(L=round(float(np.median(a[:, 0])), 1), a=round(float(np.median(a[:, 1])), 1),
                      b=round(float(np.median(a[:, 2])), 1), L10=round(float(np.percentile(a[:, 0], 10)), 1),
                      L90=round(float(np.percentile(a[:, 0], 90)), 1))
    return out
if __name__ == "__main__":
    spec = json.load(open(sys.argv[1]))
    db = {}
    for name, d in spec.items():
        db[name] = dict(dist_km=d["dist_km"], cond=d["cond"], s=sample(d["photo"], d["boxes"]))
        print(name, d["dist_km"], d["cond"])
        for k, v in db[name]["s"].items(): print("   ", k, v)
    json.dump(db, open("../cache/boxes_out.json", "w"), indent=1)
EOF

Write: elements/snowpeak/src/paint.py
"""paint.py -- translate a snow-peak G-buffer into a painted pixel image.

Pipeline (each stage is a function you can call or replace):

  1. coverage   supersampled G-buffer -> per-pixel snow fraction; snow decided by fraction, then
                cleaned so snow reads as shapes, never salt-and-pepper (aerial student's failure)
  2. material   sky / snow / ice (bare glacier) / rock / scree / meadow / forest
  3. family     lit or shade (sun n.l and cast shadow); never dithered across (observation student)
  4. step       a designed form coordinate inside each family: plateau-lit snow, core + sky-lit shade,
                gullies a step down, ribs and sun-facing crests a step up (aerial cavity tone)
  5. band       distance band with dithered seams; far bands collapse to fewer steps
  6. palette    colour = physical light on the material at that step's representative n.l, then
                the band's haze (Koschmieder: tau = exp(-d / D_h)).  The palette IS the light: one
                index canvas re-lights for any hour or haze by swapping the palette.

    canvas = index(G, P)             # dict of per-pixel int arrays
    rgb    = colour(canvas, G, P, L) # L = light state (sun, sky, haze)
"""
import numpy as np
from scipy import ndimage as ndi

MATS = ["sky", "snow", "ice", "rock", "scree", "meadow", "forest", "water"]
M = {k: i for i, k in enumerate(MATS)}
# steps per family (shade, lit)
NSTEP = {"snow": (3, 3), "ice": (2, 2), "rock": (2, 3), "scree": (2, 2), "meadow": (2, 2),
         "forest": (2, 2), "water": (1, 1), "sky": (1, 1)}

DEFAULT = dict(
    snow_thr=0.5,          # pixel is snow when this fraction of its supersamples is snow
    clean_iters=2,
    min_island=2,          # snow/rock islands smaller than this (px) are absorbed, unless ribs
    bands_km=(4, 10, 25, 55),
    seam=0.06,             # band-edge jitter, fraction of distance (aerial: 6% = 1-2 px dither)
    cav_k=0.9,             # curvature threshold in units of metres-per-pixel for gully/rib steps
    lit_min=0.06,          # n.l above which a sunlit pixel is in the lit family
)

BAYER4 = np.array([[0, 8, 2, 10], [12, 4, 14, 6], [3, 11, 1, 9], [15, 7, 13, 5]]) / 16 + 1 / 32


def bayer(h, w):
    return np.tile(BAYER4, (h // 4 + 1, w // 4 + 1))[:h, :w]


def clustered_noise(h, w, rng):
    c = rng.uniform(size=((h + 1) // 2 + 1, (w + 1) // 2 + 1))
    c = np.repeat(np.repeat(c, 2, 0), 2, 1)[:h, :w]
    off = rng.integers(0, 2, size=h)
    for y in range(h):
        c[y] = np.roll(c[y], off[y])
    return 0.55 * c + 0.45 * rng.uniform(size=(h, w))


def majority_clean(z, iters=1, protect=None):
    z = z.copy()
    for _ in range(iters):
        l = np.roll(z, 1, 1); r = np.roll(z, -1, 1); u = np.roll(z, 1, 0); d = np.roll(z, -1, 0)
        iso = (l == r) & (z != l) & (u == d) & (z != u)
        if protect is not None:
            iso &= ~protect
        z = np.where(iso, l, z)
    return z


def down(a, k, how="mean"):
    H, W = a.shape[0] // k, a.shape[1] // k
    a = a[:H * k, :W * k].reshape(H, k, W, k, *a.shape[2:])
    if how == "mean":
        return a.mean(axis=(1, 3))
    if how == "min":
        return a.min(axis=(1, 3))
    if how == "max":
        return a.max(axis=(1, 3))
    return a[:, k // 2, :, k // 2]            # centre sample


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 1-2: coverage and material
def material(Gs, k, P):
    """Gs: supersampled G-buffer (k x native). returns native material map + per-pixel fields"""
    sky_s = Gs["sky"]
    snow_s = ((Gs["cover"] > 0.5) | (Gs["glacier"] > 0)) & ~sky_s
    ice_s = (Gs["glacier"] == 2) & ~sky_s
    f_sky = down(sky_s.astype(float), k)
    f_snow = down(snow_s.astype(float), k)
    f_ice = down(ice_s.astype(float), k)
    f_tree = down(np.where(sky_s, 0, Gs["trees"]), k)
    f_bare = down(np.where(sky_s, 0, Gs["bare"]), k)
    H, W = f_sky.shape
    mat = np.full((H, W), M["rock"], np.int16)
    terr = f_sky < 0.5
    mat[~terr] = M["sky"]
    veg = terr & (f_tree > 0.5)
    mat[veg] = M["forest"]
    z = down(np.where(sky_s, 0, Gs["z"]), k)
    meadow = terr & ~veg & (f_bare < 0.3) & (z < 2000) & (f_tree < 0.5)
    mat[meadow] = M["meadow"]
    snowfrac = f_snow / np.maximum(1 - f_sky, 1e-3)
    sn = terr & (snowfrac >= P["snow_thr"])
    mat[sn] = M["snow"]
    mat[sn & (f_ice / np.maximum(f_snow, 1e-3) > 0.5)] = M["ice"]
    # clean: single pixels that disagree with both pairs of neighbours
    mat = majority_clean(mat, P["clean_iters"], protect=~terr)
    return mat, dict(snowfrac=snowfrac, terr=terr)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 3-4: family and step
def family_step(mat, G, P):
    ndl = G["ndl"]; lit = (ndl > P["lit_min"]) & (G["sunlit"] > 0.5)
    up = G["normal"][..., 2]
    H, W = mat.shape
    step = np.zeros((H, W), np.int16)
    mpp = np.maximum(G["dist"], 1) * G["pix_rad"]                 # metres per pixel on the ground
    cav = G["curv"] < -P["cav_k"] * np.clip(mpp * 0.25, 1.5, 30)
    rib = G["curv"] > P["cav_k"] * np.clip(mpp * 0.25, 1.5, 30)
    for name in MATS:
        m = mat == M[name]
        if not m.any():
            continue
        ns, nl = NSTEP[name]
        # lit: plateau that saturates early (observation student: granite scatters broadly)
        xl = np.clip((ndl - P["lit_min"]) / 0.55, 0, 1) ** 0.7 * (nl - 1 + 0.49)
        # shade: core next to the terminator, sky-lit (upward-facing) deeper in
        xs = np.clip((up - 0.35) / 0.55, 0, 1) * (ns - 1 + 0.49)
        core = (ndl > -0.25) & (ndl <= P["lit_min"])
        xs = np.where(core & (G["sunlit"] > 0.5), np.minimum(xs, 0.4), xs)
        x = np.where(lit, xl, xs)
        s = np.floor(x).astype(np.int16)
        s = np.where(cav & m, np.maximum(s - 1, 0), s)
        s = np.where(rib & lit & m, np.minimum(s + 1, np.where(lit, nl, ns) - 1), s)
        step[m] = s[m]
    fam = lit.astype(np.int16)
    return fam, step, dict(frac=None)


def bands(G, P, rng=None):
    d = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["dist"]) / 1000
    thr = bayer(*d.shape)
    dj = d * (1 + (thr - 0.5) * 2 * P["seam"])
    return np.digitize(dj, P["bands_km"]).astype(np.int16)


def collapse(fam, step, band, mat):
    """aerial perspective removes steps, not only contrast: far bands keep 2 per family, the
    farthest 1 lit + 1 shade (aerial student's band collapse)"""
    s = step.copy()
    s = np.where(band >= 3, np.minimum(s, 1), s)
    s = np.where(band >= 4, np.where(fam == 1, np.maximum(s, 1), 0), s)
    return s


def index(G, Gs, k, P=None):
    P = dict(DEFAULT, **(P or {}))
    mat, info = material(Gs, k, P)
    fam, step, _ = family_step(mat, G, P)
    band = bands(G, P)
    step = collapse(fam, step, band, mat)
    fam[mat == M["sky"]] = 1; step[mat == M["sky"]] = 0
    return dict(mat=mat, fam=fam, step=step, band=band, **info)


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 6: palette = light
ALBEDO = {  # linear RGB, measured/estimated (LESSONS part 1 has sources)
    "snow": np.array([0.86, 0.88, 0.90]), "firn": np.array([0.72, 0.73, 0.74]),
    "ice": np.array([0.36, 0.44, 0.50]), "rock": np.array([0.13, 0.115, 0.11]),
    "granite": np.array([0.30, 0.29, 0.27]), "scree": np.array([0.20, 0.19, 0.18]),
    "meadow": np.array([0.16, 0.14, 0.08]), "forest": np.array([0.035, 0.05, 0.04]),
    "water": np.array([0.04, 0.06, 0.08]),
}


def srgb(x):
    x = np.clip(x, 0, 1)
    return np.where(x <= 0.0031308, 12.92 * x, 1.055 * x ** (1 / 2.4) - 0.055)


def light_state(sun_el, sun_az, haze_km=60.0, exposure=1.0, sky_zenith=None, sky_horizon=None,
                airlight=None, warm=None):
    """a light: sun colour by elevation (warm and weak near the horizon), sky ambient, haze."""
    e = max(sun_el, -1.5)
    t = np.clip(e / 25, 0, 1)
    sun = np.array([1.0, 0.72 + 0.26 * t, 0.45 + 0.52 * t]) * (0.35 + 0.95 * np.clip(e / 20, 0, 1) ** 0.5)
    if warm is not None:
        sun = sun * np.array(warm)
    skyamb = np.array([0.30, 0.40, 0.60]) * (0.35 + 0.65 * t)
    zen = np.array(sky_zenith if sky_zenith is not None else [0.18, 0.30, 0.62]) * (0.4 + 0.6 * t)
    hor = np.array(sky_horizon if sky_horizon is not None else [0.55, 0.62, 0.75]) * (0.5 + 0.5 * t)
    air = np.array(airlight if airlight is not None else hor)
    return dict(sun=sun, skyamb=skyamb, zenith=zen, horizon=hor, air=air, haze_km=haze_km,
                exposure=exposure, el=sun_el, az=sun_az)


# representative n.l / sky view for each step (the palette is computed at these, not per pixel)
def step_light(name, fam, s):
    ns, nl = NSTEP[name]
    if fam == 1:
        ndl = 0.18 + 0.72 * (s + 0.5) / nl
        sky = 0.75
    else:
        ndl = 0.0
        sky = 0.35 + 0.6 * (s + 0.5) / ns
    return ndl, sky


def base_colour(name, fam, s, L, rock_albedo="rock"):
    alb = ALBEDO[rock_albedo if name == "rock" else name]
    ndl, sky = step_light(name, fam, s)
    direct = L["sun"] * ndl if fam == 1 else 0.0
    bounce = 0.10 * L["sun"] * (name in ("snow", "ice")) * (fam == 0)     # snow lights its own shadows
    return alb * (direct + L["skyamb"] * sky + bounce) * L["exposure"]


def colour(C, G, L, rock_albedo="rock", band_km=(2, 7, 17, 40, 80), subject_clear=0.0):
    """C: index canvas. band_km: representative distance for each band (for haze).
    subject_clear: 0..1 removes that fraction of haze from the snow/rock of the peak (Ferrari's
    'haze compresses what is behind, not the subject')."""
    H, W = C["mat"].shape
    out = np.zeros((H, W, 3))
    # sky: zenith to horizon by elevation angle of the ray
    elev = np.degrees(np.arcsin(np.clip(G["D"][..., 2], -1, 1)))
    tt = np.clip(elev / 25, 0, 1) ** 0.6
    skyc = L["horizon"][None, None] * (1 - tt[..., None]) + L["zenith"][None, None] * tt[..., None]
    out[:] = skyc
    pal = {}
    for name in MATS:
        if name == "sky":
            continue
        m0 = C["mat"] == M[name]
        if not m0.any():
            continue
        ns, nl = NSTEP[name]
        for fam in (0, 1):
            for s in range(nl if fam else ns):
                for b in range(len(band_km)):
                    m = m0 & (C["fam"] == fam) & (C["step"] == s) & (C["band"] == b)
                    if not m.any():
                        continue
                    c = base_colour(name, fam, s, L, rock_albedo)
                    tau = np.exp(-band_km[b] / L["haze_km"])
                    if name in ("snow", "ice", "rock") and subject_clear > 0:
                        tau = tau + (1 - tau) * subject_clear
                    c = c * tau + L["air"] * (1 - tau)
                    pal[(name, fam, s, b)] = c
                    out[m] = c
    return (srgb(out) * 255).astype(np.uint8), pal
Write: elements/snowpeak/src/render.py
"""render.py -- paint a fitted scene and set it beside its photo.

    python3 render.py rainier_ne_july summer 320 [z_sl]
writes stages/r_<shot>_<season>.png : photo (box-downsampled to the same pixels) | painting, at 3x
"""
import numpy as np, sys, os, json
from PIL import Image
import scene, paint

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))


def native_from_super(Gs, k, W, H, hfov):
    G = {}
    D = paint.down(Gs["D"], k, "centre"); G["D"] = D / np.linalg.norm(D, axis=-1, keepdims=True)
    sky = paint.down(Gs["sky"].astype(float), k) > 0.5
    G["sky"] = sky
    for key in ("ndl", "sunlit", "curv", "z", "cover", "hs", "cornice", "trees", "bare", "slope", "sx", "I"):
        a = np.where(Gs["sky"], np.nan, Gs[key].astype(float))
        with np.errstate(all="ignore"):
            v = np.nanmean(a[:H * k, :W * k].reshape(H, k, W, k), axis=(1, 3))
        G[key] = np.nan_to_num(v)
    n = np.where(Gs["sky"][..., None], np.nan, Gs["normal"])
    with np.errstate(all="ignore"):
        nn = np.nanmean(n[:H * k, :W * k].reshape(H, k, W, k, 3), axis=(1, 3))
    nn = np.nan_to_num(nn); nn[..., 2] += 1e-6
    G["normal"] = nn / np.linalg.norm(nn, axis=-1, keepdims=True)
    d = np.where(Gs["sky"], np.inf, Gs["dist"])
    G["dist"] = np.where(sky, np.inf, np.min(d[:H * k, :W * k].reshape(H, k, W, k), axis=(1, 3)))
    G["glacier"] = paint.down(Gs["glacier"].astype(float), k, "centre")
    G["pix_rad"] = np.radians(hfov) / W
    return G


def run(shot, season, W=320, k=3, season_over=None, light=None, P=None, sun=None, colour_kw=None):
    S = scene.Scene(shot, W=W * k)
    S.snow(season, season_over=season_over)
    Gs = S.gbuffer(sun=sun)
    H = S.H // k
    G = native_from_super(Gs, k, W, H, S.C.hfov)
    C = paint.index(G, Gs, k, P)
    az, el = S.sun_used
    L = light or paint.light_state(el, az)
    rgb, pal = paint.colour(C, G, L, **(colour_kw or {}))
    return S, G, C, rgb, pal


def photo_native(S, W, H):
    return np.asarray(Image.open(S.s["photo"]).convert("RGB").resize((W, H), Image.BOX))


def pair(S, rgb, out, scale=3):
    H, W = rgb.shape[:2]
    ph = photo_native(S, W, H)
    im = np.concatenate([ph, np.full((H, 2, 3), 255, np.uint8), rgb], 1)
    Image.fromarray(im).resize((im.shape[1] * scale, H * scale), Image.NEAREST).save(out)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    shot, season = sys.argv[1], sys.argv[2]
    W = int(sys.argv[3]) if len(sys.argv) > 3 else 320
    so = dict(z_sl=float(sys.argv[4])) if len(sys.argv) > 4 else None
    S, G, C, rgb, pal = run(shot, season, W, season_over=so)
    tag = sys.argv[5] if len(sys.argv) > 5 else ""
    pair(S, rgb, os.path.join(HERE, f"../stages/r_{shot}_{season}{tag}.png"))
    print("colours", len(pal))

import numpy as np, paint
from skimage.color import rgb2lab
def L(c): return np.round(rgb2lab(paint.srgb(np.array(c))[None,None])[0,0],1)
def show(Ls, km=(2,7,17,40,80)):
    for name in ("snow","ice","rock","scree","forest","meadow"):
        ns,nl=paint.NSTEP[name]
        for fam in (0,1):
            row=[]
            for s in range(nl if fam else ns):
                c=paint.base_colour(name,fam,s,Ls)
                row.append(str(L(c)[0])+"/"+str(L(c)[2]))
            print(f"{name:7s} {'lit ' if fam else 'shade'}", " ".join(row))
    print("sky zen",L(Ls["zenith"]),"hor",L(Ls["horizon"]))
if __name__=="__main__":
    show(paint.light_state(64,153))

    mat = majority_clean(mat, P["clean_iters"], protect=~terr)

    """sea-level-equivalent haze path (m): aerosol density falls as exp(-h / Ha) (Ha ~ 1-2 km, the
    boundary layer), so a ray from hc to ht crosses dist * mean(exp(-h/Ha)) of sea-level haze.
    This is why summits stand clear above hazy foothills."""
    dh = ht - hc
    small = np.abs(dh) < 1.0
    mean = np.where(small, np.exp(-hc / Ha),
                    Ha * (np.exp(-hc / Ha) - np.exp(-ht / Ha)) / np.where(small, 1.0, dh))
    return dist * mean


def bands(G, P, rng=None):
    d = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["deff"]) / 1000''')

Bash: Band haze by ridge layer; re-render
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("paint.py").read()
s=s.replace('''def bands(G, P, rng=None):
    d = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["deff"]) / 1000
    thr = bayer(*d.shape)
    dj = d * (1 + (thr - 0.5) * 2 * P["seam"])
    return np.digitize(dj, P["bands_km"]).astype(np.int16)''','''def bands(G, P, rng=None):
    """haze band per RIDGE LAYER, not per pixel: walk each column top-down; a depth jump
    (nearer by > layer_jump) starts a new layer, and the whole layer segment takes the band of the
    haze path at its crest.  Seams then sit on ridge crests, where a painter puts them, instead of
    wandering across a valley floor as a dithered smear (the aerial student's 'smoke' seams)."""
    d = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["deff"]) / 1000
    dist = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["dist"])
    H, W = d.shape
    raw = np.digitize(d, P["bands_km"]).astype(np.int16)
    out = raw.copy()
    jump = P["layer_jump"]
    for x in range(W):
        cur = None; prev = None
        for y in range(H):
            if G["sky"][y, x]:
                cur = None; prev = None; continue
            if cur is None or dist[y, x] * jump < prev:
                cur = raw[y, x]
            else:
                cur = max(min(cur, raw[y, x] + 1), raw[y, x] - 1) if P["layer_slack"] else cur
            out[y, x] = cur
            prev = dist[y, x]
    return out''')
s=s.replace('''    seam=0.06,''','''    seam=0.06,
    layer_jump=1.25,       # nearer by this ratio = a new ridge layer (for haze bands)
    layer_slack=1,         # a layer may drift one band from its crest before being re-banded''')
open("paint.py","w").write(s)
EOF

Bash: Mode-filtered haze bands; re-render

s=s[:a]+'''def mode_filter(z, size=5, iters=2, mask=None):
    """majority vote in a window (integers >= 0): turns ragged seams into clean shapes"""
    z = z.copy(); K = int(z.max()) + 1
    for _ in range(iters):
        votes = np.stack([ndi.uniform_filter((z == k).astype(float), size) for k in range(K)])
        nz = votes.argmax(0).astype(z.dtype)
        z = np.where(mask, nz, z) if mask is not None else nz
    return z


def bands(G, P, rng=None):
    """haze bands from the sea-level-equivalent haze path, as clean flat shapes: quantize, then a
    majority filter so each seam is a crisp run (no dither: dithered seams read as smoke)."""
    d = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["deff"]) / 1000
    raw = np.digitize(d, P["bands_km"]).astype(np.int16)
    terr = ~G["sky"]
    out = mode_filter(np.where(terr, raw, 0), P["band_mode"], 2, mask=terr)
    return np.where(terr, out, raw)


'''+s[b:]

    layer_jump=1.25,''')

**Library (coordinator's note, mid-morning).** Read Imhof, *Cartographic Relief Presentation*
(scanned PDF, no text layer; pdf page = book page + 18): ch. 9 §8 aerial perspective (p172-173:
"dark tones grow lighter and light tones are subdued... contrast progressively reduced";
cartographers *sharpen* contrast toward high ground, subdue lowland), §18-19 (p190-193: shadow
colour grey-blue / blue-violet; **shading on snow must keep the same strength as on the rest of the
terrain** or snow ridges read as plateaus; shadows on snow pure blue), ch. 11 §3-5 (p243-248:
Figure 170 "normal group of forms": gullies and intervening ribs, parallel, acute-angled
"poplar-tree" branching, steepest near the top, triangular frontal facets; every gully ends in a
debris cone at 30-40 deg, fine at the top, blocks at the toe). Ruskin, *Modern Painters IV*
(text layer): the value problem ("the distant rocks of the upper peaks are themselves, when in
light, paler than white paper... yet as compared with their snow, so dark"), and rock showing "in
black points like arrow-heads through the snow, where their ridges are left projecting by the
avalanche channels." Both go straight into the method: gullies hold snow, ribs are arrowheads.

**Camera fits, round 2.** Control-point init (put the summit on its photo pixel, scan fov) fixed
local optima. rainier_ne_july (Silver Queen, 21 km): excellent, foreground ridges line up
(`stages/fit_rainier_ne_july.png`). sea_rainier_clear (Seattle tower, 94 km): good. rainier_w_sept
(Rainier20200906): FAILED. The GPS point sits in a hollow; snapping to the highest point within
300 m and 1.5 km, and a 9x9 km position search (`possearch.py`) all gave weak, ambiguous scores
(-0.52..-0.54). The tag is probably not where the photo was taken. Parked; used as a September
reference by eye only.

**Measurements** (`src/boxes.py`, `src/measure.py`, `cache/boxes_out.json`, `cache/measure.json`).

- Rainier 21 km, clear noon July: lit snow L*91, sky at the peak 64, sky high 47 (b* -29),
  rock wall 36-54 (b* -11: haze is already blue), forest at 5 km 21 (b* -18).
- Rainier 94 km, clear May evening: lit snow 65 vs sky 58 (+7.5); foothill forest band 45 (-12 vs sky).
- Rainier 96 km, August evening (sun just set at the camera, still on the summit): snow 73,
  rock 69, sky 73.6. Snow = sky. This is not haze, it is weak alpenglow: at dusk the lit snow sinks
  to the sky's value.
- Koschmieder fits in luminance: forest at ~60-70 km gives tau ~0.45, i.e. D_h ~ 80 km on the
  clear day. But the forest at 5-8 km under the Silver Queen camera (at 2129 m) is nearly
  un-hazed (L*14.6 vs intrinsic ~12). Uniform haze cannot fit both. **Aerosol density falls
  with height (scale ~1.5-2 km)**: the painter now uses a sea-level-equivalent haze path
  `eff_dist(dist, cam_z, target_z, Ha)`. This one change put the forest at the photo's value
  (22.6 -> 14.7) and is the physical reason summits stand clear above hazy foothills.

**Painter v1-v9** (`src/paint.py`, `src/render.py`, `stages/r_rainier_ne_july_summer_v*.png`).

- v1: silhouette right from the DEM; everything else grey and hazy, snow dashes, ice blue patches.
- `dbg_matL.py`: photo L* under each painted material vs mine. The single best instrument so far:
  forest 25 vs 14.5, meadow 47 vs 28, scree 64 vs 55. Albedos recalibrated.
- v7: seam dither between steps (clustered thresholds). Haze bands dithered at 6% of distance
  made a smoky checker field across the valley (the aerial student's trap, again).
- v8: per-column "ridge layer" bands: vertical streaks. v9: quantize the haze path, then a 5x5
  majority filter: clean flat haze shapes; the near ridge now separates dark against the bluer
  valley, as in the photo.
- Snow colour: blue-white against the photo's warm white; cause was a blue-tinted snow albedo and
  too much sky in the lit ramp. Fixed.
EOF

Bash: Occlusion-layer bands, wider lit ramp, sky; re-render
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("paint.py").read()
a=s.index("def bands(G, P, rng=None):"); b=s.index("def collapse(")
s=s[:a]+'''def bands(G, P, rng=None):
    """haze bands per OCCLUSION LAYER: crest lines (depth jumps > layer_jump) cut the terrain into
    regions; each region is one flat haze band, the band of its median haze path.  Ferrari's ridge
    layers: each flat, lighter with distance, seams on crests.  Dithered seams read as smoke (aerial
    student); per-pixel bands wander across valley floors."""
    d = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["deff"]) / 1000
    dist = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["dist"])
    terr = ~G["sky"]
    raw = np.digitize(d, P["bands_km"]).astype(np.int16)
    up = np.roll(dist, 1, 0); up[0] = dist[0]
    lf = np.roll(dist, 1, 1); lf[:, 0] = dist[:, 0]
    rt = np.roll(dist, -1, 1); rt[:, -1] = dist[:, -1]
    edge = terr & ((up > dist * P["layer_jump"]) | (lf > dist * P["layer_jump"]) | (rt > dist * P["layer_jump"]))
    edge = ndi.binary_dilation(edge, structure=np.array([[0, 0, 0], [1, 1, 1], [0, 0, 0]]))
    lab, n = ndi.label(terr & ~edge)
    out = raw.copy()
    if n:
        med = ndi.median(d, lab, np.arange(1, n + 1))
        bnd = np.digitize(med, P["bands_km"]).astype(np.int16)
        area = ndi.sum(np.ones_like(lab), lab, np.arange(1, n + 1))
        big = area >= P["layer_min_area"]
        m = lab > 0
        out[m] = np.where(big[lab[m] - 1], bnd[lab[m] - 1], raw[m])
    # edge pixels take the band of the region just below them (they are the crest's front)
    dn = np.roll(out, -1, 0)
    out = np.where(edge, dn, out)
    return np.where(terr, out, raw)


'''+s[b:]
s=s.replace('''    layer_jump=1.25,''','''    layer_jump=1.2,
    layer_min_area=30,     # smaller regions keep per-pixel bands
    lit_lo=0.35,           # the lit ramp starts here (Imhof: shade form on snow at full strength)''')
s=s.replace('''xl = np.clip((ndl - P["lit_min"]) / P["lit_span"], 0, 1)''','''xl = np.clip((ndl - P["lit_lo"]) / P["lit_span"], 0, 1)''')
s=s.replace('''    ledge_min_len=6,''','''    ledge_min_len=12,''')
s=s.replace("lit_span=0.75, lit_gamma=1.0","lit_span=0.6, lit_gamma=1.0")
s=s.replace("tt = np.clip(elev / 25, 0, 1) ** 0.6","tt = np.clip(elev / L.get('sky_span', 14), 0, 1) ** 0.7")
s=s.replace("[0.29, 0.40, 0.53]","[0.26, 0.40, 0.56]")

s=open("paint.py").read()

def band_centres(edges=BAND_EDGES):
    e = (0.0,) + tuple(edges) + (edges[-1] * 1.5,)
    return tuple((e[i] + e[i + 1]) / 2 for i in range(len(e) - 1))


def bands(G, P, rng=None):
    """haze bands: the sea-level-equivalent haze path quantized finely (each band a small value
    step, so a band seam is a gentle contour, like the photo's gradient, not a hard shape), then a
    3x3 majority so seams are clean runs.  Coarse bands with big jumps made blobs (v9)."""
    d = np.where(G["sky"], 1e12, G["deff"]) / 1000
    raw = np.digitize(d, P["bands_km"]).astype(np.int16)
    terr = ~G["sky"]
    out = mode_filter(np.where(terr, raw, 0), 3, 2, mask=terr)
    return np.where(terr, out, raw)

    """aerial perspective removes steps, not only contrast: beyond collapse_km[0] of haze path each
    family keeps 2 steps, beyond collapse_km[1] one lit + one shade (aerial student's band collapse)"""
    km = np.array(band_centres(P["bands_km"]))[band]
    s = step.copy()
    s = np.where(km >= P["collapse_km"][0], np.minimum(s, 1), s)

    out = np.zeros((H, W, 3))''')
# forest: more steps and stronger shading
s=s.replace('"forest": (2, 2)','"forest": (2, 3)')
open("paint.py","w").write(s)
EOF

Bash: Thickness-based sliver rule, bluer forest
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("paint.py").read()

        sliver = (rows < 2) & (area < P["ledge_min_len"])''','''        cols = np.array([s_[1].stop - s_[1].start for s_ in sl])
        thick = area / np.maximum(cols, 1)                      # mean rows per column
        sliver = (thick < 1.5) & (area < P["ledge_min_len"] * 1.5)''')

Bash: Fall-line smoothing of snow cover; re-render
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("render.py").read()
s=s.replace('''    H = S.H // k
    G = native_from_super(Gs, k, W, H, S.C.hfov)''','''    H = S.H // k
    add_fall_line(Gs, S.C)
    G = native_from_super(Gs, k, W, H, S.C.hfov)''')
s=s.replace('''def add_haze_path(''','''def add_fall_line(Gs, C, step_m=None):
    """screen-space direction of steepest descent at each pixel (unit vector, pixels)"""
    n = Gs["normal"]
    d = np.stack([n[..., 2] * n[..., 0], n[..., 2] * n[..., 1], n[..., 2] ** 2 - 1], -1)
    d /= np.linalg.norm(d, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
    dist = np.where(Gs["sky"], 1.0, Gs["dist"])
    s = np.maximum(dist * np.radians(C.hfov) / C.W * 2, 5.0)          # ~2 px of ground
    x0, y0 = C.project(Gs["e"], Gs["n"], Gs["z"])
    x1, y1 = C.project(Gs["e"] + d[..., 0] * s, Gs["n"] + d[..., 1] * s, Gs["z"] + d[..., 2] * s)
    v = np.stack([x1 - x0, y1 - y0], -1)
    v /= np.linalg.norm(v, axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
    Gs["fall"] = np.where(Gs["sky"][..., None], 0.0, v)
    return Gs


def add_haze_path(''')
open("render.py","w").write(s)

s=open("paint.py").read()
s=s.replace('''    snow_s = ((Gs["cover"] > 0.5) | (Gs["glacier"] > 0)) & ~sky_s''','''    cov_s = np.where(sky_s, 0.0, np.maximum(Gs["cover"], (Gs["glacier"] > 0).astype(float)))
    if P["fall_len"] > 0 and "fall" in Gs:
        cov_s = fall_smooth(cov_s, Gs["fall"], P["fall_len"] * k, ~sky_s)
    snow_s = (cov_s > 0.5) & ~sky_s''')
s=s.replace('''# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 1-2: coverage and material''','''# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 1-2: coverage and material
def fall_smooth(a, fall, L, valid, n=None):
    """average a along the screen-space fall line over +-L px (line integral convolution): snow
    tongues and rock ribs stretch down the slope, as gullies and ribs do (Imhof fig. 170)"""
    H, W = a.shape
    n = n or max(2, int(L))
    yy, xx = np.mgrid[0:H, 0:W].astype(float)
    acc = a.copy(); wsum = np.ones_like(a)
    for sgn in (1, -1):
        x = xx.copy(); y = yy.copy()
        for i in range(n):
            ix = np.clip(np.round(x).astype(int), 0, W - 1); iy = np.clip(np.round(y).astype(int), 0, H - 1)
            fx = fall[iy, ix, 0]; fy = fall[iy, ix, 1]
            x = x + sgn * fx * L / n; y = y + sgn * fy * L / n
            ix = np.clip(np.round(x).astype(int), 0, W - 1); iy = np.clip(np.round(y).astype(int), 0, H - 1)
            ok = valid[iy, ix]
            w = ok * (1 - i / n)
            acc += a[iy, ix] * w; wsum += w
    return acc / wsum

''')
s=s.replace('''    ledge_min_len=12,''','''    ledge_min_len=12,
    fall_len=1.5,          # native px of fall-line smoothing of snow cover before the threshold''')
s=s.replace("lit_lo=0.35,","lit_lo=0.2,").replace("lit_span=0.6, lit_gamma=1.0","lit_span=0.7, lit_gamma=1.0")
open("paint.py","w").write(s)
EOF

Bash: Add remote lidar loader and test at Stuart
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; cat >> dem.py <<'EOF'


# ------------------------------------------------------------------ 1 m lidar, read remotely
LIDAR = {"stuart": ["WA_EasternCascades_2019_B19"], "dragontail": ["WA_EasternCascades_2019_B19"],
         "glacierpeak": ["WA_GlacierPeak_2014"], "rainier": ["WA_PierceCounty_2020_A20"],
         "olympus": ["WA_Olympic_Peninsula_2013", "WA_Olympic_Peninsula_C3_2017", "WA_Olympic_Peninsula_Lidar_2017_B17"],
         "baker": ["WA_MtBaker_2015"]}
S3 = "https://prd-tnm.s3.amazonaws.com/"


def _list_tiles(project):
    import urllib.request, re
    fn = os.path.join(CACHE, f"tiles_{project}.txt")
    if os.path.exists(fn):
        return open(fn).read().split()
    keys, token = [], None
    while True:
        u = S3 + f"?list-type=2&prefix=StagedProducts/Elevation/1m/Projects/{project}/TIFF/"
        if token:
            import urllib.parse
            u += "&continuation-token=" + urllib.parse.quote(token)
        x = urllib.request.urlopen(u).read().decode()
        keys += re.findall(r"<Key>([^<]+\.tif)</Key>", x)
        m = re.search(r"<NextContinuationToken>([^<]+)</NextContinuationToken>", x)
        if not m:
            break
        token = m.group(1)
    open(fn, "w").write("\n".join(keys))
    return keys


def load_lidar(e0, e1, n0, n1, res, projects, tag):
    """1 m USGS lidar DEM over a UTM box, read over HTTP from the COG overviews (no tile download),
    resampled to `res`.  Cells with no lidar are NaN (callers fill from the 10 m DEM)."""
    import re
    fn = os.path.join(CACHE, f"lidar_{tag}_{res:g}.npz")
    if os.path.exists(fn):
        d = np.load(fn)
        return dict(Z=d["Z"], x0=float(d["x0"]), y0=float(d["y0"]), res=float(d["res"]))
    W = int(round((e1 - e0) / res)); H = int(round((n1 - n0) / res))
    transform = from_origin(e0, n1, res, res)
    Z = np.full((H, W), np.nan, np.float32)
    with rasterio.Env(GDAL_DISABLE_READDIR_ON_OPEN="EMPTY_DIR", CPL_VSIL_CURL_ALLOWED_EXTENSIONS=".tif"):
        for proj in projects:
            for key in _list_tiles(proj):
                m = re.search(r"x(\d+)y(\d+)", key)
                if not m:
                    continue
                tx, ty = int(m.group(1)) * 10000, int(m.group(2)) * 10000
                if tx > e1 or tx + 10000 < e0 or ty < n0 or ty - 10000 > n1:
                    continue
                with rasterio.open("/vsicurl/" + S3 + key, overview_level=None) as src:
                    tmp = np.full((H, W), np.nan, np.float32)
                    reproject(rasterio.band(src, 1), tmp, src_transform=src.transform, src_crs=src.crs,
                              dst_transform=transform, dst_crs=UTM, resampling=Resampling.average,
                              src_nodata=src.nodata, dst_nodata=np.nan)
                    ok = np.isfinite(tmp) & ~np.isfinite(Z)
                    Z[ok] = tmp[ok]
                print("  lidar tile", key.split("/")[-1], flush=True)
    np.savez_compressed(fn, Z=Z, x0=e0, y0=n1, res=res)
    return dict(Z=Z, x0=float(e0), y0=float(n1), res=float(res))
EOF

Bash: Two-level DEM ray casting with lidar
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("cam.py").read()
s=s.replace('''@njit(parallel=True, cache=True, fastmath=True)
def _cast(Z, x0, y0, res, ce, cn, cz, D, far, reff, out_t):''','''@njit(cache=True, fastmath=True)
def _h2(Z, x0, y0, res, F, fx0, fy0, fres, px, py):
    g = _h(F, fx0, fy0, fres, px, py)
    if g > -1e8:
        return g
    return _h(Z, x0, y0, res, px, py)


@njit(parallel=True, cache=True, fastmath=True)
def _cast2(Z, x0, y0, res, F, fx0, fy0, fres, ce, cn, cz, D, far, reff, out_t):
    """as _cast, but a fine grid F (lidar) overrides Z where it exists"""
    H, W = D.shape[0], D.shape[1]
    for i in prange(H):
        for j in range(W):
            dx, dy, dz = D[i, j, 0], D[i, j, 1], D[i, j, 2]
            hor = np.sqrt(dx * dx + dy * dy) + 1e-9
            t = 2.0; tp = 0.0; hit = -1.0
            while t < far:
                px = ce + dx * t; py = cn + dy * t
                th = t * hor
                pz = cz + dz * t + th * th / (2 * reff)
                g = _h2(Z, x0, y0, res, F, fx0, fy0, fres, px, py)
                if g < -1e8:
                    break
                above = pz - g
                if above <= 0:
                    lo = tp; hi = t
                    for _ in range(20):
                        m = 0.5 * (lo + hi)
                        qx = ce + dx * m; qy = cn + dy * m; qh = m * hor
                        qz = cz + dz * m + qh * qh / (2 * reff)
                        if qz - _h2(Z, x0, y0, res, F, fx0, fy0, fres, qx, qy) <= 0:
                            hi = m
                        else:
                            lo = m
                    hit = hi
                    break
                tp = t
                step = above * 0.5
                r = res
                if _h(F, fx0, fy0, fres, px, py) > -1e8:
                    r = fres
                smin = 0.35 * r + t * 0.0002
                if step < smin:
                    step = smin
                if step > 0.02 * t + 4 * r:
                    step = 0.02 * t + 4 * r
                t += step
            out_t[i, j] = hit


@njit(parallel=True, cache=True, fastmath=True)
def _cast(Z, x0, y0, res, ce, cn, cz, D, far, reff, out_t):''')
s=s.replace('''    t = np.zeros((C.H, C.W))
    _cast(T["Z"].astype(np.float32), T["x0"], T["y0"], T["res"], C.e, C.n, C.z, D, far, R_EFF, t)''','''    t = np.zeros((C.H, C.W))
    F = T.get("fine")
    if F is not None:
        _cast2(T["Z"].astype(np.float32), T["x0"], T["y0"], T["res"], F["Z"].astype(np.float32),
               F["x0"], F["y0"], F["res"], C.e, C.n, C.z, D, far, R_EFF, t)
    else:
        _cast(T["Z"].astype(np.float32), T["x0"], T["y0"], T["res"], C.e, C.n, C.z, D, far, R_EFF, t)''')
s=s.replace('''    z = map_coordinates(T["Z"], [r.ravel(), c.ravel()], order=1, mode="nearest").reshape(r.shape)''','''    z = map_coordinates(T["Z"], [r.ravel(), c.ravel()], order=1, mode="nearest").reshape(r.shape)
    if F is not None:
        rf = (F["y0"] - n) / F["res"] - 0.5; cf = (e - F["x0"]) / F["res"] - 0.5
        zf = map_coordinates(F["Z"], [rf.ravel(), cf.ravel()], order=1, mode="constant", cval=np.nan).reshape(r.shape)
        z = np.where(np.isfinite(zf), zf, z)''')
open("cam.py","w").write(s)

Bash: Read gbuffer code
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; sed -n 40,100p scene.py

# output:
np.savez_compressed(fn, **{k: np.asarray(v, np.float32) for k, v in prep.items() if k != "P"})
        self.prep = prep
        se = dict(snowmodel.SEASONS[season], **(season_over or {}))
        self.S = snowmodel.apply(prep, se, P)
        self.season = season
        return self.S

    def gbuffer(self, sun=None):
        G = cam.cast(self.T, self.C)
        T = self.T; Tp = self.Tp
        sl, asp, n = snowmodel.fields(T["Z"], T["res"])
        def samp(A, r, c, order=1):
            return map_coordinates(np.asarray(A, np.float32), [r.ravel(), c.ravel()], order=order,
                                   mode="nearest").reshape(r.shape)
        r, c = G["r"], G["c"]
        G["normal"] = np.stack([samp(n[..., k], r, c) for k in range(3)], -1)
        G["normal"] /= np.linalg.norm(G["normal"], axis=-1, keepdims=True) + 1e-9
        G["slope"] = samp(sl, r, c)
        for k in ("ice", "trees", "bare"):
            G[k] = samp(T[k], r, c)
        # snow fields from the peak sub-box (outside it: none, the sub-box is chosen to hold all snow)
        rp = (Tp["y0"] - G["n"]) / Tp["res"] - 0.5; cp = (G["e"] - Tp["x0"]) / Tp["res"] - 0.5
        inside = (rp > 0) & (rp < Tp["Z"].shape[0] - 1) & (cp > 0) & (cp < Tp["Z"].shape[1] - 1) & ~G["sky"]
        G["rp"], G["cp"], G["inside"] = rp, cp, inside
        self.G = G
        self.resample()
        # sun shadow at each hit point, by marching the peak box (fine) then the view box
        az, el = sun or self.sun
        self.sun_used = (az, el)
        shp = snowmodel.shadow(Tp["Z"], Tp["res"], az, el, maxd=20000.0) if el > -3 else np.zeros_like(Tp["Z"])
        shv = snowmodel.shadow(T["Z"][::2, ::2], T["res"] * 2, az, el, maxd=30000.0) if el > -3 else None
        sh_view = samp(shv, r / 2, c / 2) if shv is not None else np.zeros(r.shape)
        G["sunlit"] = np.where(inside, samp(shp, rp, cp), sh_view)
        G["sunvec"] = sunmod.vec(az, max(el, 0.5))
        G["ndl"] = G["normal"] @ G["sunvec"]
        # sub-pixel curvature for gully/rib reading
        cv = snowmodel.curvature(T["Z"], T["res"], 90)
        G["curv"] = samp(cv, r, c)
        self.G = G
        return G

    def resample(self):
        """(re)sample the current snow state into the G-buffer (cheap; call after snow())"""
        G = self.G; rp, cp, inside = G["rp"], G["cp"], G["inside"]
        def samp(A, order=1):
            return map_coordinates(np.asarray(A, np.float32), [rp.ravel(), cp.ravel()], order=order,
                                   mode="nearest").reshape(rp.shape)
        for k in ("hs", "cover", "cornice", "I", "sx"):
            G[k] = np.where(inside, samp(self.S[k]), 0.0)
        G["glacier"] = np.where(inside, samp(self.S["glacier"], 0), 0)
        return G

            # 1 m lidar resampled to lidar_res over the peak sub-box; gaps filled from the 10 m DEM
            half = s.get("lidar_half_km", 4) * 1000; lr = s["lidar"]
            e, n = dem.ll2utm(lat, lon)
            box = (round(e - half), round(e + half), round(n - half), round(n + half))
            L = dem.load_lidar(*box, lr, dem.LIDAR[s["peak"]], f"{s['peak']}_{int(half)}")

## Round 2: designed light, and matching the photo itself

The coordinator's read (and David's): round 1 approximated the photos; the goal is to *match* them,
form first (Dragontail's real silhouette, where its flank turns dark, where the gullies run), then
detail. This round is about that, and about "masses first, then marks inside the mass".

### 2a. Masses first, then marks (`paint.shade_designed`, `paint.mass_marks`)

- **Mass vs mark.** An object is a *mass* if it covers > 3 % of the frame **and** is > 12 m in
  the world (walls, stacks, platforms); everything else (talus blocks, boulders) is a *mark* and
  keeps per-facet light. My first version also treated a near foreground block as a mass and it
  swallowed the talus.
- **Mass light from the landform, not the facets.** The mass's lit/shadow split comes from the
  landform-scale normal (the world position blurred over ~6 % of the frame, then its normal) and the
  landform-scale share of cast shadow, then `design_masses` (no island < 2.5 % of frame). Inside a
  lit mass every facet stays in the lit family; inside a shadow mass every facet stays in the shadow
  family, shaped by **directional skylight** (up tilted toward a bright-sky direction).
- **Marks inside the lit mass are tall runs, not specks.** A facet turned away from the sun becomes
  a shadow-family mark only if its connected run is ≥ 6 px tall and ≥ 2.2× taller than wide (the
  dark rib/gully strokes of a sunlit wall); the hackly entablature uses any shape. Small pockets
  (< 10 px) stay lit. Crack lines along joint steps are off by default (they drew closed loops
  round every cell — "crazy paving" — at 8×).
- **A director override** (`mass_design={"basalt": "shadow", "basalt_face": "lit"}`) for when the
  photo says which mass is dark.
- **Column bow and taper** (`column_surface(bow=, taper=)`): each column bends as one curve of its
  own sign and phase and narrows upward.

Stage pairs: `stages/iter/granite_wall_d1–d10.png`, `stages/iter/basalt_colonnade_d1–d5.png`.
The granite wall now has one lit face and one dark flank with rib strokes in the light
(`granite_wall_d10`), but it is still my invented mountain, not Dragontail. The basalt got bowed
columns and a dark overhanging entablature (`basalt_colonnade_d5`), but the columns still read as
clean pale planks, not the photo's rounded, streaked pillars — **not there**.

Based on the search results, I found information about the Norwegian α-β model developed by Lied and Bakkehøi for snow avalanche runout prediction.

The alpha-beta model (Lied and Bakkehoi, 1980) is based on the idea that the runout angle alpha is proportional to the beta angle, which is a measure of the mean slope angle. The statistical α/β model predicts the extreme run-out distance for a snow avalanche solely as a function of topography.

Regarding the typical alpha angle values, Lied and Bakkehøi (1980) surveyed major avalanches in Norway and found that the angle from the starting point to the run–out point is on average 33°, although values as low as 18° and as high as 49° have been found.

The model also shows that in the equation presented by Lied and Bakkehøi (1980) R = 0.95 and SD = 2.3°. The alpha–beta regression runout model (Bakkehoi et al., 1983, Lied and Bakkehoi, 1980, Lied et al., 1989), initially applied in Norway by the Norwegian Geotechnical Institute (NGI) and applied in other parts of the world, is well suited to topographic mapping.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about transverse crevasse spacing and depth:

## Crevasse Spacing

Transverse crevasse spacings on temperate glaciers are variable, ranging from 30 m to 96 m. More specifically, initial transverse crevasses can have spacings ranging from 57 to 66 m for cold ice.

There's also a quantitative relationship between spacing and depth: crevasse spacings average 2.8 times the mean crevasse depth, which is about 26 meters.

## Crevasse Depth

Regarding depth, most dry crevasses do not exceed a depth of 30 m. However, the depth can range from a few metres to 50 metres and more in alpine glaciers, and in very cold ice at high latitudes, crevasse depth can exceed 30 m, with some observed to be 45 m.

Crevasse depth is controlled by the compressive stresses within the ice, with ice overburden pressure increasing with depth and forcing the crack shut.

Based on the search results, here's what I found about Mount Rainier's glacier area and changes:

## Glacier Area in 2015

Mount Rainier National Park contains 29 named glacial features which cover an area of 30.4 square miles (78.8 km²) as of 2015 according to Beason's research.

## Total Area Change (1896-2015)

Between 1896 and 2015, Mount Rainier as a whole has had an overall decrease in glacial area of 20.1 square miles (52.1 km²), or an annual decrease of 0.17 square miles per year (0.44 km²/year) according to Beason (2017) and George and Beason (2017).

## Historical Context

The research indicates that the glacial area has been reduced from around 130 km² to around 75, with most of the loss occurring between 1895 and 1970, a period when there was very little human-caused global warming.

The Beason (2017) study referenced is the National Park Service's comprehensive glacier inventory documenting these changes at Mount Rainier over more than a century.

Bash: Debug Stuart snow cover and lighting

import numpy as np, sys, scene
from PIL import Image
shot=sys.argv[1]; season=sys.argv[2]; z=float(sys.argv[3])
S=scene.Scene(shot,W=960); S.snow(season,season_over=dict(z_sl=z)); G=S.gbuffer()
c=np.where(G["sky"],0.3,np.clip(G["cover"],0,1)); c=np.where(G["glacier"]>0,1.0,c)
hs=np.where(G["sky"],0,np.clip(G["hs"]/2,0,1))
ndl=np.where(G["sky"],0,np.clip(G["ndl"],0,1)*G["sunlit"])
ph=np.asarray(Image.open(S.s["photo"]).convert("L").resize((960,S.H),Image.LANCZOS))/255.
out=np.concatenate([ph,c,hs,ndl],0)
Image.fromarray((out*255).astype(np.uint8)).save(f"../stages/dbg_cover_{shot}.png")
print("cover>0.5 frac on terrain", (c[~G["sky"]]>0.5).mean(), "hs max", G["hs"].max())
EOF

    shd = shd * (1 + P["k_gully"] * gully)
    hs_g = _route(Z, hs_w, shd, res, P["pit_cap"])''')

170:def family_step(mat, G, P):
def family_step(mat, G, P):
    ndl = G["ndl"]
    sf = G.get("shadefrac", np.zeros_like(ndl))
    lit = sf < 0.5
    thr = clustered_noise(*ndl.shape, np.random.default_rng(P.get("seed", 1)))
    up = G["normal"][..., 2]
    H, W = mat.shape
    step = np.zeros((H, W), np.int16)
    mpp = np.maximum(G["dist"], 1) * G["pix_rad"]                 # metres per pixel on the ground
    cav = G["curv"] < -P["cav_k"] * np.clip(mpp * 0.25, 1.5, 30)
    rib = G["curv"] > P["cav_k"] * np.clip(mpp * 0.25, 1.5, 30)
    for name in MATS:
        m = mat == M[name]
        if not m.any():
            continue
        ns, nl = NSTEP[name]
        # lit: plateau that saturates early (observation student: granite scatters broadly)
        xl = np.clip((ndl - P["lit_lo"]) / P["lit_span"], 0, 1) ** P["lit_gamma"] * (nl - 1 + 0.49)
        # shade: core next to the terminator, sky-lit (upward-facing) deeper in
        xs = np.clip((up - 0.35) / 0.55, 0, 1) * (ns - 1 + 0.49)
        core = (ndl > -0.25) & (ndl <= P["lit_min"])
        xs = np.where(core & (G["sunlit"] > 0.5), np.minimum(xs, 0.4), xs)
        # sub-pixel shade pulls a lit pixel down (ribs and gullies narrower than a pixel read as
        # texture); sub-pixel light lifts a shade pixel
        xl = xl - P["subpix_k"] * sf * (nl - 1)
        xs = xs + P["subpix_k"] * (1 - sf) * (ns - 1) * 0.5
        rel = G.get("relief", np.zeros_like(ndl))
        rk = P["relief_k"].get(name, 0.0)
        xl = xl + rk * rel / (np.std(rel[m]) + 1e-6) * 0.5
        xs = xs + rk * rel / (np.std(rel[m]) + 1e-6) * 0.3
        x = np.where(lit, xl, xs)
        # flat planes with a dithered seam at each half step (aerial student's flat_bands);
        # clustered thresholds so seams read as strings and clumps, not salt-and-pepper
        f = np.floor(x); frac = x - f
        seam = P["seam_w"]
        t = np.clip((frac - (0.5 - seam / 2)) / seam, 0, 1)
        s = (f + (t > thr)).astype(np.int16)
        s = np.minimum(s, np.where(lit, nl - 1, ns - 1))
        s = np.where(cav & m, np.maximum(s - 1, 0), s)
        s = np.where(rib & lit & m, np.minimum(s + 1, np.where(lit, nl, ns) - 1), s)
        step[m] = s[m]
    fam = lit.astype(np.int16)
    return fam, step, dict(frac=None)


def eff_dist(dist, hc, ht, Ha=1500.0):
    """sea-level-equivalent haze path (m): aerosol density falls as exp(-h / Ha) (Ha ~ 1-2 km, the
    boundary layer), so a ray from hc to ht crosses dist * mean(exp(-h/Ha)) of sea-level haze.
    This is why summits stand clear above hazy foothills."""
    dh = ht - hc
    small = np.abs(dh) < 1.0
    mean = np.where(small, np.exp(-hc / Ha),
                    Ha * (np.exp(-hc / Ha) - np.exp(-ht / Ha)) / np.where(small, 1.0, dh))
    return dist * mean
Bash: Quantile-keyed steps blended with absolute; re-render
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("paint.py").read()
a=s.index("def family_step(mat, G, P):"); b=s.index("def eff_dist(")
s=s[:a]+'''def rank_steps(score, m, shares):
    """map score over mask m to a continuous step coordinate whose integer parts have the given
    shares (quantile keying: the ground student's 'key each family by its own quantiles', the rock
    student's 'value economy taken from the photo')."""
    x = np.zeros_like(score)
    if m.sum() < 4:
        return x
    v = score[m]
    order = np.argsort(np.argsort(v)); r = (order + 0.5) / len(v)           # rank in 0..1
    edges = np.concatenate([[0], np.cumsum(shares)]); edges /= edges[-1]
    k = np.clip(np.searchsorted(edges, r, side="right") - 1, 0, len(shares) - 1)
    frac = (r - edges[k]) / np.maximum(edges[k + 1] - edges[k], 1e-9)
    x[m] = k + frac
    return x


def family_step(mat, G, P):
    """lit/shade family per pixel (from the sub-pixel shade fraction), then a step inside the family.
    The step coordinate blends an absolute light value (n.l, sky view) with a rank-keyed one, so
    every material shows its forms at a designed value economy, even under flat high sun."""
    ndl = G["ndl"]
    sf = G.get("shadefrac", np.zeros_like(ndl))
    lit = sf < 0.5
    thr = clustered_noise(*ndl.shape, np.random.default_rng(P.get("seed", 1)))
    up = G["normal"][..., 2]
    rel = G.get("relief", np.zeros_like(ndl))
    H, W = mat.shape
    step = np.zeros((H, W), np.int16)
    mpp = np.maximum(G["dist"], 1) * G["pix_rad"]
    cav = G["curv"] < -P["cav_k"] * np.clip(mpp * 0.25, 1.5, 30)
    rib = G["curv"] > P["cav_k"] * np.clip(mpp * 0.25, 1.5, 30)
    for name in MATS:
        m = mat == M[name]
        if not m.any():
            continue
        ns, nl = NSTEP[name]
        rk = P["relief_k"].get(name, 0.0)
        q = P["key_q"].get(name, 0.0)
        sh = P["shares"].get(name, {})
        # absolute coordinates
        xl = np.clip((ndl - P["lit_lo"]) / P["lit_span"], 0, 1) ** P["lit_gamma"] * (nl - 1 + 0.49)
        xs = np.clip((up - 0.35) / 0.55, 0, 1) * (ns - 1 + 0.49)
        core = (ndl > -0.25) & (ndl <= P["lit_min"])
        xs = np.where(core & (G["sunlit"] > 0.5), np.minimum(xs, 0.4), xs)
        # scores for keying: light + local relief - sub-pixel shade
        sd = np.std(rel[m]) + 1e-6
        scl = ndl + rk * rel / sd * 0.15 - P["subpix_k"] * 0.15 * sf
        scs = up + rk * rel / sd * 0.15 + 0.3 * (1 - sf)
        ml = m & lit; ms = m & ~lit
        rl = rank_steps(scl, ml, sh.get("lit", [1] * nl))
        rs = rank_steps(scs, ms, sh.get("shade", [1] * ns))
        x = np.where(lit, (1 - q) * xl + q * rl, (1 - q) * xs + q * rs)
        f = np.floor(x); frac = x - f
        seam = P["seam_w"]
        t = np.clip((frac - (0.5 - seam / 2)) / seam, 0, 1)
        s = (f + (t > thr)).astype(np.int16)
        s = np.clip(s, 0, np.where(lit, nl - 1, ns - 1))
        s = np.where(cav & m, np.maximum(s - 1, 0), s)
        s = np.where(rib & lit & m, np.minimum(s + 1, np.where(lit, nl, ns) - 1), s)
        step[m] = s[m]
    fam = lit.astype(np.int16)
    return fam, step, dict(frac=None)


'''+s[b:]

Bash: Firn retention on glaciers; re-render GP
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("snowmodel.py").read()
s=s.replace('''    rem = prep["hs_grav"] - melt''','''    rem = prep["hs_grav"] - melt
    # on glacier ice below ~35 deg, winter snow is not shed by sluffing the way it is on rock (the
    # glacier surface is itself the product of retained snow): use the wind-redistributed depth.
    # This makes the firn line (upper glacier white, lower glacier grey ice) fall at the ELA.
    on_gl = (prep["ice"] > 0.5) & (slope < 35)
    rem = np.where(on_gl, np.maximum(rem, prep["hs_wind"] - melt), rem)''')
open("snowmodel.py","w").write(s)
EOF

Bash: Read Scene constructor
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; sed -n 1,35p scene.py

# output:
"""scene.py -- one view of a real peak: DEM, snow model, camera, per-pixel G-buffer.

    S = Scene("rainier_ne_july", W=320)        # uses cache/cams.json + fitcam.SHOTS
    S.snow("summer")                            # runs snowmodel on the peak sub-box
    G = S.gbuffer()                             # per-pixel fields for the painter
"""
import numpy as np, json, os
from scipy.ndimage import map_coordinates
import dem, cam, fitcam, snowmodel, sun as sunmod

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))


class Scene:
    def __init__(self, shot, W=320, H=None, cams=None, snow_half_km=11, snow_res=15, cam_override=None):
        self.shot = shot
        s = fitcam.SHOTS[shot]; self.s = s
        c = (cams or json.load(open(os.path.join(HERE, "../cache/cams.json"))))[shot]
        if cam_override:
            c = dict(c, **cam_override)
        ce, cn, pe, pn, bx = fitcam.box(s)
        self.T = dem.load_box(*[round(v) for v in bx], s["res"], tag=shot)
        from PIL import Image
        pw, ph = Image.open(s["photo"]).size
        self.W = W; self.H = H or round(W * ph / pw)
        self.C = cam.Camera(c["e"], c["n"], c["z"], c["az"], c["pitch"], c["hfov"], self.W, self.H, c["roll"])
        lat, lon, _ = dem.PEAKS[s["peak"]]
        self.lat, self.lon = lat, lon
        if s.get("lidar"):
            # 1 m lidar resampled to lidar_res over the peak sub-box; gaps filled from the 10 m DEM
            half = s.get("lidar_half_km", 4) * 1000; lr = s["lidar"]
            e, n = dem.ll2utm(lat, lon)
