Write: elements/snowpeak/src/sheets.py
"""sheets.py -- labelled sweeps: peak x season x distance x hour/haze, from real terrain.

    python3 sheets.py stuart          # one sheet per peak: rows distance x season, cols light
    python3 sheets.py all

A synthetic view: stand `dist` km from the summit on the real bearing of a real vantage (Seattle
for Rainier, Ingalls/Longs Pass for Stuart, Hurricane Ridge for Olympus, Colchuck Lake for the
Dragontail ridge), on the highest ground within a few hundred metres, and look at the summit.
"""
import numpy as np, os, sys, time
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont
import dem, scene, render, paint, cam, sun as sunmod

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
OUT = os.path.join(HERE, "../sheets")

PEAKSPEC = {
    # bearing FROM the peak TO the camera (deg), massif width (m) to frame, rock albedo, label
    "rainier": dict(bearing=330, width=17000, rock="andesite", label="Mount Rainier (4392 m) from the NW (Seattle side)"),
    "stuart": dict(bearing=200, width=7000, rock="granite", label="Mount Stuart (2870 m) from the SSW (Longs Pass side)"),
    "olympus": dict(bearing=40, width=12000, rock="rock", label="Mount Olympus (2432 m) from the NE (Hurricane Ridge side)"),
    "dragontail": dict(bearing=345, width=4500, rock="granite", label="Dragontail-Colchuck ridge (2694 m) from the N (Colchuck Lake side)"),
}
DISTS = (5, 20, 80)
SEASONS = {"sept": ((9, 15), "September minimum"), "spring": ((5, 25), "late May (spring)")}
# light variants: (label, clock hour or 'set-5min', haze_km, haze_scale_m, warm)
LIGHTS = [("clear noon", 13.0, 70.0, 2000.0, None),
          ("clear evening", "set-60", 70.0, 2000.0, None),
          ("hazy afternoon", 16.0, 18.0, 2600.0, None),
          ("alpenglow", "set-6", 55.0, 2000.0, (1.0, 0.62, 0.55))]


def sunset_hour(lat, lon, mo, d):
    for m in range(16 * 60, 22 * 60):
        az, el = sunmod.local(lat, lon, 2021, mo, d, m // 60, m % 60)
        if el < -0.83:
            return (m - 1) / 60.0
    return 20.0


def make_view(peak, dist_km, W=240, H=135, k=3):
    lat, lon, zs = dem.PEAKS[peak]
    pe, pn = dem.ll2utm(lat, lon)
    ps = PEAKSPEC[peak]
    b = np.radians(ps["bearing"]); d = dist_km * 1000
    ce, cn = pe + np.sin(b) * d, pn + np.cos(b) * d
    res = 10 if dist_km <= 6 else (20 if dist_km <= 25 else 50)
    pad = max(6000, ps["width"] * 0.8)
    e0, e1 = min(ce, pe) - pad, max(ce, pe) + pad
    n0, n1 = min(cn, pn) - pad, max(cn, pn) + pad
    T = dem.load_box(round(e0), round(e1), round(n0), round(n1), res, tag=f"sheet_{peak}_{dist_km}")
    # stand on the highest cell within r of the nominal point
    r = int(min(0.06 * d, 1500) / res)
    r0 = int((T["y0"] - cn) / res); c0 = int((ce - T["x0"]) / res)
    win = T["Z"][max(r0 - r, 0):r0 + r + 1, max(c0 - r, 0):c0 + r + 1]
    i, j = np.unravel_index(np.argmax(win), win.shape)
    cn = cn - (i - min(r, r0)) * res; ce = ce + (j - min(r, c0)) * res
    cz = float(win[i, j]) + 3
    dd = np.hypot(pe - ce, pn - cn)
    az = np.degrees(np.arctan2(pe - ce, pn - cn))
    el_s = np.degrees(np.arctan2(zs - cz - dd * dd / (2 * cam.R_EFF), dd))
    hfov = float(np.clip(np.degrees(2 * np.arctan(ps["width"] / 2 / dd)), 4, 75))
    vfov = hfov * H / W
    pitch = el_s - 0.22 * vfov          # summit ~28% down from the top of the frame
    spec = dict(peak=peak, res=res, time=(2021, 9, 15, 13, 0), rock=ps["rock"], aspect=(W, H),
                camera=dict(e=ce, n=cn, z=cz, az=az, pitch=pitch, hfov=hfov, roll=0.0))
    S = scene.Scene(f"sheet_{peak}_{dist_km}", W=W * k, H=H * k, spec=spec, T=T)
    return S, dict(dist=dd, hfov=hfov, cz=cz)


def cell(S, season, light, W, H, k=3):
    (mo, d), _ = SEASONS[season]
    lab, hour, haze_km, Ha, warm = light
    if isinstance(hour, str):
        hour = sunset_hour(S.lat, S.lon, mo, d) - float(hour.split("-")[1]) / 60
    h = int(hour); mi = int(round((hour - h) * 60)) % 60
    az, el = sunmod.local(S.lat, S.lon, 2021, mo, d, h, mi)
    se = "sept" if season == "sept" else "spring"
    so = dict(z_sl=2150) if season == "sept" else dict(z_sl=1250)
    S.snow(se, season_over=so)
    Gs = S.gbuffer(sun=(az, el)); S.resample(); Gs = S.G
    render.add_fall_line(Gs, S.C)
    G = render.native_from_super(Gs, k, W, H, S.C.hfov)
    render.add_haze_path(G, S.C.z, Ha)
    P = {}
    C = paint.index(G, Gs, k, P)
    hazy = haze_km < 30
    L = paint.light_state(el, az, haze_km=haze_km, warm=warm,
                          airlight=[0.42, 0.44, 0.47] if hazy else None,
                          sky_horizon=[0.40, 0.43, 0.48] if hazy else None,
                          sky_zenith=[0.16, 0.26, 0.45] if hazy else None)
    if warm is not None:   # alpenglow: the sky near the horizon goes rose, the zenith deepens
        L["horizon"] = np.array([0.42, 0.30, 0.33]); L["zenith"] = np.array([0.07, 0.10, 0.24])
        L["air"] = np.array([0.30, 0.25, 0.30]); L["skyamb"] = np.array([0.10, 0.10, 0.18])
    rgb, pal = paint.colour(C, G, L, rock_albedo=S.s["rock"])
    return rgb, (az, el), len(pal)


def font(sz):
    for f in ("/System/Library/Fonts/Supplemental/Arial.ttf", "/System/Library/Fonts/Helvetica.ttc"):
        if os.path.exists(f):
            return ImageFont.truetype(f, sz)
    return ImageFont.load_default()


def sheet(peak, W=240, H=135, scale=2):
    t0 = time.time()
    rows = []
    for dk in DISTS:
        S, info = make_view(peak, dk, W, H)
        for season in SEASONS:
            cells = []
            for light in LIGHTS:
                rgb, sunae, nc = cell(S, season, light, W, H)
                cells.append((rgb, light[0], sunae, nc))
            rows.append((dk, season, info, cells))
            print(peak, dk, season, f"{time.time() - t0:.0f}s", flush=True)
    cw, ch = W * scale, H * scale
    lw, top, gap = 190, 60, 6
    img = Image.new("RGB", (lw + len(LIGHTS) * (cw + gap), top + len(rows) * (ch + gap)), (24, 24, 28))
    dr = ImageDraw.Draw(img)
    dr.text((10, 8), PEAKSPEC[peak]["label"] + " -- real terrain (USGS 3DEP), snow model, painter", fill=(235, 235, 235), font=font(20))
    for ci, light in enumerate(LIGHTS):
        dr.text((lw + ci * (cw + gap) + 4, 36), f"{light[0]}  (haze {light[2]:.0f} km)", fill=(200, 200, 200), font=font(15))
    for ri, (dk, season, info, cells) in enumerate(rows):
        y = top + ri * (ch + gap)
        dr.text((8, y + 6), f"{dk} km", fill=(235, 235, 235), font=font(22))
        dr.text((8, y + 36), SEASONS[season][1], fill=(200, 200, 200), font=font(14))
        dr.text((8, y + 56), f"hfov {info['hfov']:.0f} deg\ncamera {info['cz']:.0f} m", fill=(150, 150, 150), font=font(12))
        for ci, (rgb, lab, (az, el), nc) in enumerate(cells):
            x = lw + ci * (cw + gap)
            img.paste(Image.fromarray(rgb).resize((cw, ch), Image.NEAREST), (x, y))
            dr.text((x + 4, y + ch - 16), f"sun {az:.0f}/{el:.0f}  {nc} col", fill=(255, 255, 255), font=font(11))
    os.makedirs(OUT, exist_ok=True)
    fn = os.path.join(OUT, f"A_{peak}_distance_season_light.png")
    img.save(fn)
    print("wrote", fn)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    ps = sys.argv[1:] or ["stuart"]
    if ps == ["all"]:
        ps = list(PEAKSPEC)
    for p in ps:
        sheet(p)

# ------------------------------------------------------------------ marks: crevasses, cornices
def crevasse_mask(Gs, P):
    """transverse crevasses on glacier ice where it steepens (extending flow): open cracks
    crev_open m wide at spacing crev_space m (30-96 m measured on temperate glaciers), running across
    the flow.  Phase = position along the local flow direction; a slow warp bends the lines into
    arcs, as crevasses bow downglacier toward the margins."""
    n = Gs["normal"]
    fx, fy = n[..., 0], n[..., 1]
    fl = np.hypot(fx, fy) + 1e-9
    fx, fy = fx / fl, fy / fl
    e, nn = Gs["e"], Gs["n"]
    warp = 18 * np.sin(e / 230.0) * np.cos(nn / 310.0)
    ph = (e * fx + nn * fy + warp) / P["crev_space"]
    fr = ph - np.floor(ph)
    sl = Gs["slope"]
    on = (Gs["glacier"] > 0) & (sl > P["crev_slope"][0]) & (sl < P["crev_slope"][1]) & ~Gs["sky"]
    return on & (fr < P["crev_open"] / P["crev_space"])


def marks(mat, fam, step, G, Gs, k, P):
    if P["crevasses"]:
        cm = down(crevasse_mask(Gs, P).astype(float), k)
        onice = np.isin(mat, [M["snow"], M["ice"]])
        c = onice & (cm > P["crev_min_frac"])
        step[c] = 0; fam[c] = 0
        # the crack's lip catches light: the pixel above a crevasse (uphill on screen) goes lit top
        lip = np.roll(c, -1, 0) & onice & ~c & (fam == 1)
        ns, nl = NSTEP["snow"]
        step[lip] = nl - 1
    if P["cornices"] and "cornice" in G:
        cor = (G["cornice"] > 0.3) & (mat != M["sky"])
        mat[cor] = M["snow"]; fam[cor] = 1; step[cor] = NSTEP["snow"][1] - 1
        below = np.roll(cor, 1, 0) & ~cor & (mat != M["sky"])
        mat[below] = M["snow"]; fam[below] = 0; step[below] = 0          # the curl's own shadow''')
s=s.replace('''    canopy_hide=1.0,''','''    canopy_hide=1.0,
    crevasses=True, crev_space=45.0, crev_open=6.0, crev_slope=(11, 38), crev_min_frac=0.34,
    cornices=True,''')

Write: elements/snowpeak/src/vocab.py
"""vocab.py -- close-ups of the snow-on-rock vocabulary, on small synthetic terrains run through the
SAME snow model and painter as the real peaks (so the close rules and the far rules are one rule).

  gully fill   a 55-deg face cut by parallel gullies that branch upward (Imhof fig. 170)
  ledge bands  a stepped face: 70-deg risers, 20-deg treads (bedding / sheeting joints)
  cornice      an asymmetric ridge, wind across it: lip on the lee crest, scoured windward slope
  glacier edge a glacier tongue in a trough: lateral moraines, crevasse bands where it steepens,
               grey bare ice below the firn line

    python3 vocab.py            -> sheets/B_vocabulary_scale_season.png
"""
import numpy as np, os
from PIL import Image, ImageDraw
from scipy import ndimage as ndi
import scene, render, paint, sheets as SH, sun as sunmod

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))


def grid(size_m, res):
    n = int(size_m / res)
    y, x = np.mgrid[0:n, 0:n].astype(float) * res          # x east, y south (row)
    return x, y, n


def noise(n, res, scale_m, seed, amp):
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    a = ndi.gaussian_filter(rng.normal(size=(n, n)), scale_m / res)
    return a / (a.std() + 1e-9) * amp


def terrain(kind, size_m=600.0, res=1.0, seed=3):
    x, y, n = grid(size_m, res)
    ice = np.zeros((n, n), np.float32)
    if kind == "gully":
        # face rising to the north (row 0 = north = high), 55 deg, gullies spaced ~45 m whose count
        # doubles upward (poplar branching: channels split toward their heads)
        h = (size_m - y) * np.tan(np.radians(52))
        lam = 60 - 30 * (1 - y / size_m)
        wob = noise(n, res, 40, seed, 8)
        ph = (x + wob) / lam
        rib = np.abs(np.sin(np.pi * ph)) ** 0.7
        Z = h + 14 * rib + noise(n, res, 6, seed + 1, 1.5)
        Z += 400 * np.clip((y - 0.8 * size_m) / (0.2 * size_m), 0, 1) ** 2 * 0   # (flat base omitted)
    elif kind == "ledge":
        H = (size_m - y) * np.tan(np.radians(48))
        step = 12.0
        f = (H / step) % 1.0
        # within each 12 m of height: 70 % riser (steep), 30 % tread (gentle): remap height
        Z = H + step * 0.35 * np.where(f < 0.3, f / 0.3 - f, (1 - f) * 0.3 / 0.7 - (f - 0.3) * 0)
        Z = ndi.gaussian_filter(Z, 0.8) + noise(n, res, 25, seed, 3)
    elif kind == "cornice":
        # ridge running E-W; windward (south) 28 deg, lee (north) 50 deg, crest undulating
        c = size_m * 0.45 + noise(n, res, 60, seed, 1)[:, :1].repeat(n, 1) * 0 + 12 * np.sin(x / 70)
        d = y - c
        Z = np.where(d > 0, -d * np.tan(np.radians(28)), d * np.tan(np.radians(50)) * -1 * -1)
        Z = np.where(d > 0, 300 - d * np.tan(np.radians(28)), 300 + d * np.tan(np.radians(50)))
        Z = ndi.gaussian_filter(Z, 1.5) + noise(n, res, 20, seed, 2)
    elif kind == "glacier":
        # a trough falling to the south; glacier tongue down its axis, steepening in the middle (icefall)
        cx = size_m / 2 + 25 * np.sin(y / 160)
        dx = x - cx
        long = (size_m - y) * np.tan(np.radians(9)) + 60 * np.clip((y - 250) / 120, 0, 1)
        Z = long + 0.004 * dx ** 2 * 3 + noise(n, res, 30, seed, 2)
        halfw = 110 - 50 * y / size_m
        tongue = np.abs(dx) < halfw
        Z = np.where(tongue, Z - 6 * (1 - (dx / halfw) ** 2), Z)
        # lateral moraines: sharp ridges just outside the ice edge
        Z += 9 * np.exp(-((np.abs(dx) - halfw - 8) / 6) ** 2)
        ice[tongue & (y < size_m * 0.85)] = 1.0
        Z = Z + 300
    Z = Z - Z.min() + 1800
    return dict(Z=Z.astype(np.float32), ice=ice, trees=np.zeros_like(ice), bare=np.ones_like(ice) * 0.5,
                x0=0.0, y0=float(n * res), res=res)


def view(kind, T, W=160, H=120, k=3, dist_m=None, az=0.0, elev=25.0):
    n = T["Z"].shape[0]; size = n * T["res"]
    tx, ty = size / 2, T["y0"] - size / 2                   # target: centre of the patch
    tz = float(T["Z"][n // 2, n // 2])
    d = dist_m or size * 1.1
    a = np.radians(az)                                      # camera on bearing az FROM the target
    ce, cn = tx + np.sin(a) * d * np.cos(np.radians(elev)), ty + np.cos(a) * d * np.cos(np.radians(elev))
    cz = tz + d * np.sin(np.radians(elev))
    vaz = np.degrees(np.arctan2(tx - ce, ty - cn))
    pitch = -np.degrees(np.arctan2(cz - tz, np.hypot(tx - ce, ty - cn)))
    hfov = np.degrees(2 * np.arctan(size * 0.42 / d))
    spec = dict(peak="stuart", res=T["res"], rock="granite", aspect=(W, H), synthetic=True,
                time=(2021, 9, 15, 13, 0), camera=dict(e=ce, n=cn, z=cz, az=vaz, pitch=pitch, hfov=hfov, roll=0))
    return scene.Scene(f"vocab_{kind}", W=W * k, H=H * k, spec=spec, T=T)


VIEWS = {  # camera bearing from the patch, elevation angle, wind direction (from)
    "gully": (180, 18, 225), "ledge": (180, 15, 225), "cornice": (70, 12, 200), "glacier": (180, 35, 225),
}


def cell(S, season, W, H, k=3, wind=225):
    (mo, d), _ = SH.SEASONS[season]
    az, el = sunmod.local(47.47, -120.9, 2021, mo, d, 13, 0)
    fn = os.path.join(HERE, f"../cache/snowprep_{S.shot}.npz")
    import snowmodel
    if not hasattr(S, "prep"):
        S.prep = snowmodel.prepare(S.Tp, wind, None, 47.47, -120.9)
    se = dict(snowmodel.SEASONS["sept" if season == "sept" else "spring"],
              z_sl=2150 if season == "sept" else 1650)
    S.S = snowmodel.apply(S.prep, se)
    Gs = S.gbuffer(sun=(az, el)); S.resample(); Gs = S.G
    render.add_fall_line(Gs, S.C)
    G = render.native_from_super(Gs, k, W, H, S.C.hfov)
    render.add_haze_path(G, S.C.z, 2000)
    C = paint.index(G, Gs, k, {})
    L = paint.light_state(el, az, haze_km=70)
    rgb, pal = paint.colour(C, G, L, rock_albedo="granite")
    return rgb


def run(W=160, H=120, scale=3):
    rows = []
    for kind in ("gully", "ledge", "cornice", "glacier"):
        for res, lab in ((1.0, "1 m/px region"), (0.25, "0.25 m/px landscape")):
            size = 600 if res == 1.0 else 160
            T = terrain(kind, size, res)
            b, e, wind = VIEWS[kind]
            S = view(kind + str(res), T, W, H, az=b, elev=e)
            cells = [cell(S, s, W, H, wind=wind) for s in ("sept", "spring")]
            rows.append((kind, lab, cells))
            print(kind, res, flush=True)
    cw, ch = W * scale, H * scale
    lw, top, gap = 170, 44, 6
    img = Image.new("RGB", (lw + 2 * (cw + gap), top + len(rows) * (ch + gap)), (24, 24, 28))
    dr = ImageDraw.Draw(img)
    dr.text((10, 8), "Snow-on-rock vocabulary: synthetic terrain through the same snow model and painter", fill=(235, 235, 235), font=SH.font(18))
    for ci, s in enumerate(("September minimum", "late spring")):
        dr.text((lw + ci * (cw + gap) + 4, 28), s, fill=(200, 200, 200), font=SH.font(14))
    for ri, (kind, lab, cells) in enumerate(rows):
        y = top + ri * (ch + gap)
        dr.text((8, y + 8), kind, fill=(235, 235, 235), font=SH.font(20))
        dr.text((8, y + 36), lab, fill=(180, 180, 180), font=SH.font(13))
        for ci, rgb in enumerate(cells):
            img.paste(Image.fromarray(rgb).resize((cw, ch), Image.NEAREST), (lw + ci * (cw + gap), y))
    fn = os.path.join(HERE, "../sheets/B_vocabulary_scale_season.png")
    img.save(fn); print("wrote", fn)


if __name__ == "__main__":
    run()

s=open("sheets.py").read()
a=s.index("    # stand on the highest cell within r of the nominal point"); b=s.index("    dd = np.hypot(pe - ce, pn - cn)")
s=s[:a]+'''    # stand where the summit is visible: among local high points near the nominal point (and a
    # little further along the bearing), keep the first whose ray to the summit is not blocked
    best = None
    for push in (0.0, 0.05, -0.05, 0.1, 0.15, 0.2, 0.3):
        for side in (0.0, 0.04, -0.04, 0.08, -0.08):
            e1_ = ce + np.sin(b) * d * push + np.cos(b) * d * side
            n1_ = cn + np.cos(b) * d * push - np.sin(b) * d * side
            r = int(min(0.03 * d, 800) / res)
            r0 = int((T["y0"] - n1_) / res); c0 = int((e1_ - T["x0"]) / res)
            if r0 - r < 0 or c0 - r < 0 or r0 + r >= T["Z"].shape[0] or c0 + r >= T["Z"].shape[1]:
                continue
            win = T["Z"][r0 - r:r0 + r + 1, c0 - r:c0 + r + 1]
            i, j = np.unravel_index(np.argmax(win), win.shape)
            e2, n2, z2 = e1_ + (j - r) * res, n1_ - (i - r) * res, float(win[i, j]) + 3
            v = np.array([pe - e2, pn - n2, 0.0]); dd_ = np.hypot(v[0], v[1])
            v[2] = zs - 25 - z2 - dd_ * dd_ / (2 * cam.R_EFF)
            D = (v / np.linalg.norm(v))[None, None].astype(np.float64)
            t = np.zeros((1, 1))
            cam._cast(T["Z"].astype(np.float32), T["x0"], T["y0"], T["res"], e2, n2, z2, D, 3e5, cam.R_EFF, t)
            hit = t[0, 0]
            if hit < 0 or hit > np.linalg.norm(v) * 0.97:
                best = (e2, n2, z2); break
        if best:
            break
    if best is None:
        best = (e2, n2, z2)
    ce, cn, cz = best
'''+s[b:]
open("sheets.py","w").write(s)
EOF

# Snow peaks at distance: from real terrain to pixels

A student notebook. Subject: snowy mountains seen from miles away (Rainier, Stuart, Glacier Peak,
Olympus, the Dragontail ridge): how snow really lies on steep rock, how seasons and glaciers move it,
and how a snow peak reads through 5, 20 and 80 km of air. Then a painter that draws it, checked
against photographs of named peaks with fitted cameras.

**Where this stands, plainly.** The *form* half works: every picture here is cast from the real
USGS terrain, from the photographer's real position (camera fitted to the photo's skyline), so the
silhouettes, the big light and dark masses and where the snow sits are the real mountain's, not an
invented one. Glacier Peak (12 km, late August) and Rainier from the northeast (21 km, July) read as
those mountains beside their photos. The snow model is a stack of published processes with
numbers, portable to a simulation. The *surface* half is not at the Ferrari bar: lit snow is a
little too flat and cold, near forest reads as camouflage blobs, rock texture is a value pattern
without the structural strokes Imhof and Ruskin draw, and the vocabulary close-ups (cornice,
crevasses) are the thinnest part. Details per stage, and the open list at the end.

**The studio shoulders I stand on.**

- *Imhof, Cartographic Relief Presentation* (library/terrain): aerial perspective as contrast
  loss that must stay strongest on the high ground (ch. 9 §8); shading on snow keeps the same
  strength as on rock, or snow ridges read as plateaus (§18-19); the "normal group of forms" of a
  rock wall: parallel gullies and ribs, poplar-tree branching, debris cones at 30-40 deg (ch. 11).
- *Ruskin, Modern Painters IV* (library/rock): the value problem of snow mountains (lit distant rock
  is paler than white paper yet black against its snow), and rock ribs showing "in black points like
  arrow-heads through the snow" where avalanche channels leave them projecting.
- Studio students: the observation student's three-panel sheet and ramp coordinate with a jump at
  the terminator; the aerial student's band collapse, cavity tone and "far snow is salt-and-pepper,
  blur before you threshold"; the ground student's quantile keying of each family; the rock
  student's camera fit to a photo skyline and "value economy taken from the photo"; the snow
  student's "on mountains the rock defines the snow".
- *Tyndall* and *Lobeck* I read for glacier structure and cirque form; they inform part 1 but no
  code depends on them directly.

without; the 80 km sheet rows read a 180 x 110 km DEM box at 50 m (~8 M cells), fine. DEM tiles are
read over HTTP from USGS's cloud-optimized GeoTIFFs (the local copies were pruned for disk), then
cached in `cache/` as small npz boxes (the folder stays under ~300 MB).

---

## Part 1. Observation: how snow lies on a real mountain

### 1.1 The one idea: gravity and sun decide, wind and glaciers edit

Snow falls nearly evenly (more with height); then it moves. From far away, the pattern you see is
the *residue* of three movements, in order of how much they matter for a picture:

1. **Gravity sheds snow from steep ground** and packs it into gullies, onto ledges and into bowls
   and slope-foot cones. This is why a summer peak is dark rock laced with white *lines* (couloirs)
   and *bands* (ledges), not a white cap with a clean snowline.
2. **The sun melts unevenly**: south and west faces lose snow weeks before north faces; a gully in
   its own shadow keeps snow long after the rib beside it is bare.
3. **Wind** scours crests and exposed windward slopes and loads lee slopes, building cornices on
   lee crests. On Rainier it is the difference between a bare Liberty Cap edge and loaded bowls.
4. **Glaciers** are where snow survives from year to year; their upper parts stay white (firn) all
   summer, their lower parts turn grey bare ice by August, cut by crevasses where the ice steepens.

### 1.2 Numbers a simulation can use

| process | rule | numbers | source |
|---|---|---|---|
| accumulation with height | HS(z) = g (z - z0), capped | g ~ 0.45 m depth per 100 m above z0 ~ 300 m; Paradise (1,650 m) peak depth typically 4-6 m, record 9.3 m (1956); cap 9-14 m | NPS/NRCS SNOTEL 679 (Paradise); my fit |
| gravity: snow holding depth | SHD(slope) = c exp(-a slope); excess slides to lower neighbours (multiple flow direction), top-down | Bernhardt & Schulz 2010 default: 3.08 / 1.11 / 0.45 / 0.15 m at 30 / 45 / 60 / 75 deg. OGGM Snowslide default c = 145 m, a = 0.14 /deg: 2.2 / 0.27 / 0.03 m. **Summer photos of Rainier's walls (bare above ~45 deg) match the OGGM set**, the B&S set left the Willis Wall white | Bernhardt & Schulz 2010 GRL; OGGM/Snowslide `snd_max_exponential`; my photo test |
| steep rough rock still holds some | ledges and cracks keep a patchy cover on 50-70 deg rock | snow depth did not fall with angle up to 70 deg in snow pits on 50-65 deg Alpine walls; insulation starts above 0.2 m | Haberkorn et al. 2015/2017 (Gemsstock) |
| couloirs hold more than open slopes | side walls support a wedge of snow | holding depth x (1 + k gully), k ~ 3, gully = concavity at 25 m radius / 4 m | my fit to Stuart (photo: every white line is a gully) |
| avalanche deposits | cones at gully mouths, 5-15 m deep, last into July at 1,500 m | alpha angle (start to runout) ~ 33 deg mean, 18-49 deg | Lied & Bakkehoi 1980 |
| wind exposure | Winstral Sx: max upwind slope angle within 300 m; HS x clip(1 + 0.035 Sx, 0.15, 2.5) | crests (Sx < 0) scoured, lees (Sx > 0) loaded | Winstral, Elder & Davis 2002 |
| cornices | lee crest, exposed approach, drop > 35 deg downwind, enough snow | grow fastest at 1-2 x the threshold wind speed for drift (~1.4x best) | Vogel et al. 2012; Eckerstorfer 2013; Yu 2022-23 |
| melt | melt(z) = A(z_sl) (1 + 0.9 (z_sl - z)/km) (0.5 + 0.5 (I/I_flat)^0.8) | half air temperature (aspect-blind), half sun (clear-sky insolation with cast shadows, May-Aug) | degree-day + radiation index models (Hock 1999 in spirit); my fit |
| season snowline (flat, undisturbed) | z_sl | late May ~1,250 m; mid-July ~1,700-1,900 m; September minimum ~2,100-2,200 m (Cascades); Olympics ~200 m lower | my calibration against the dated photos below |
| glacier firn line (ELA) | above: white firn all year; below: grey bare ice by August | Rainier: Nisqually ELA ~3,180 m, Emmons ~2,800 m (2002-08); South Cascade 1,900 m; Blue Glacier (Olympus) 1,505-2,030 m, mean firn limit ~1,725 m | Hekkers 2010 (PSU); Pelto/NCGCP; UW Blue Glacier record |
| glacier extents | the map, not a model | ESA WorldCover 2021 class 70 (snow & ice) at 10 m; Rainier 78.8 km2 (2015), down 52 km2 since 1896; Olympics 25.3 km2 (2015, half of 1900) | ESA WorldCover; Beason 2017; Fountain et al. 2022 |
| crevasses | transverse, where the ice steepens (extending flow) | spacing 30-96 m on temperate glaciers, ~2.8 x depth; depth mostly < 30 m | J. Glac. "Primary transverse crevasses"; Colgan et al. |
| steep ice | faces steeper than ~42 deg inside a mapped glacier are rock walls or hanging ice | (Willis Wall, Liberty Ridge) | my photo check |

### 1.3 Light and air (measured on the fitted photos)

With a fitted camera every photo pixel has a known distance and material, so the photos become
instruments (`src/measure.py`, `src/boxes.py`; raw numbers in `cache/measure.json`, `cache/boxes_out.json`):

| photo | distance | what | L* (b*) |
|---|---|---|---|
| Rainier from Silver Queen, clear July noon | 21 km | lit snow / rock wall / forest at 5 km / sky at peak / sky high | 91 (+5) / 36-54 (-11) / 21 (-18) / 64 (-19) / 47 (-29) |
| Glacier Peak from the east, Aug 30 09:20 | 12 km | lit firn / bare ice / dacite rock / forest | 73-90 / 54-73 / 46 (-4) / 19.5 |
| Rainier from Seattle tower, clear May evening | 94 km | lit snow / shaded snow / sky at peak / foothills | 65 (+11) / 60 (+6) / 58 (-2) / 45 (-3) |
| Rainier from Elliott Bay, Aug 20:52 | 96 km | snow / rock / sky | 73 / 69 / 74 (snow = sky: weak alpenglow) |

What these say:

- **At 94 km on a clear day, lit snow is only +7 L\* over the sky** and shaded snow +2. The far
  peak is a pale shape a hair lighter than its sky, warmer (b\* +11 against -2): the snow keeps
  the sun's colour, the sky and the foothills go blue-grey. Rock at that distance is gone into the
  snow's value.
- **The foothills are the darkest thing on the horizon** (-12 L\* under the sky at 94 km) because
  they are lower, in thicker air, and forested. The peak above them is lighter than the sky.
- **Haze is not uniform.** Forest 5-8 km below the Silver Queen camera (2,129 m) is nearly
  un-hazed (L\* 14.6 against ~12 intrinsic), while forest at 60-70 km in the lowland photo fits a
  visual range of ~80 km. One Koschmieder distance can't fit both. Aerosol density falls with
  height (scale height ~1.5-2.5 km, the boundary layer), so the painter uses a sea-level-equivalent
  path: `deff = d * Ha (e^(-hc/Ha) - e^(-ht/Ha)) / (ht - hc)`. This single change put the forest at
  the photo's value (22.6 -> 14.7) and is *the* reason summits stand clear over hazy foothills.
- **At dusk the lit snow sinks to the sky's value** (96 km, sun just set at sea level): alpenglow
  is weak and warm, and the peak nearly vanishes. Imhof's rule that high ground keeps its contrast
  is a clear-air rule.
- Earth curvature with standard refraction (R_eff = R/(1-0.13)): the terrain drops d^2/(2R_eff),
  630 m at 96 km. Rainier from Seattle stands on a sunk horizon; foothills 60 km out lose their
  lower slopes behind nearer ridges. The caster bends the terrain for every ray.

### 1.4 How a snow peak changes through the year (from the dated photos)

- **Late May** (Stuart spring rows): continuous snow above ~1,300 m on shaded aspects, but ribs and
  steep faces already dark; the mountain reads as white with dark *arrowheads* (Ruskin).
- **July** (Rainier, Silver Queen): the snowline is up at ~1,900 m on open slopes; below it, snow
  survives only in gullies and avalanche cones, as white threads and wedges.
- **Late August / September minimum** (Glacier Peak; Stuart July 2008 in a low-snow year): seasonal
  snow gone from rock except couloirs and north-facing ledges; glaciers split at the firn line into
  white upper and grey lower; pumice and dacite (Glacier Peak) are pale tan, granite (Stuart) warm
  grey and lichen-dark.
- **First autumn snow** (Stuart, Oct 2024 photos): a thin veneer that sheds from steep rock at once
  and draws every ledge and bedding line; the `fresh` season option models it (not in the sheets).

---

## Part 2. Translation: the method

### 2.1 Real form first: fitted cameras on real terrain

`src/dem.py` (USGS 3DEP 1/3" and 1 m lidar, read remotely from the cloud-optimized GeoTIFFs;
ESA WorldCover for ice, trees, bare), `src/cam.py` (curved-earth ray caster, numba, two-level for
lidar), `src/fit.py` + `src/fitcam.py` (camera fit).

The camera position comes from the photo's GPS (Commons metadata, `refs/commons_meta.json`). One
panorama is cast from there; heading, pitch, fov and roll are then found by warping it, so the fit
costs image warps, not ray casts. Score: the photo's Lab edge strength along the render's
silhouettes (skyline + depth jumps); colour sky masks failed in haze (`stages/t_sk_all.png`).
Two fixes made it reliable: (1) **a control point**: put the summit on its photo pixel and scan fov,
then refine (local optima otherwise); (2) **snap** the camera to the highest ground within a few
hundred metres (photographers stand on high points; GPS tags sit in hollows).

| shot | distance | result |
|---|---|---|
| Rainier from Silver Queen Peak, July | 21 km | excellent; foreground ridges line up (`stages/fit_rainier_ne_july.png`) |
| Glacier Peak from the east, Aug 2003 | 12 km | excellent (`stages/fit_pair_sg.png`, bottom) |
| Mount Stuart from the south, July 2008 | 5.6 km | excellent (`stages/fit_pair_sg.png`, top) |
| Rainier from the Seattle tower | 94 km | good (`stages/fit_sea_rainier_clear.png`) |
| Rainier from Elliott Bay | 96 km | good (`stages/fit_sea_rainier.png`) |
| Rainier "20200906" (west side, September) | 16 km | **failed**: the GPS tag is not where the photo was taken; a 9 x 9 km position search found nothing convincing |
| Olympus from Hurricane Ridge (telephoto) | 25 km | **failed**: the fit locks onto the wrong ridge line; my summit pixel guess is probably not Olympus |

### 2.2 The snow model (`src/snowmodel.py`)

`prepare()` runs once per peak (fields, Sx, gravity routing, insolation over the melt season with
cast shadows); `apply(prep, season)` is cheap (melt, fresh snow, glacier surface state, cover).
Every rule is part 1's table. Calibration went through the photos, and each correction was forced
by a picture:

- **Holding depth.** The Bernhardt & Schulz defaults left Rainier's steep walls white in July.
  OGGM's steeper curve bared everything above ~45 deg, as the photo does (`stages/dbg_cover_crop.png`).
- **Couloirs.** With plain holding depth, Stuart kept almost no snow (1.4% of terrain vs the photo's
  white threads). Gully side-wall support (x4 holding depth in a 4 m-deep, 25 m-radius concavity)
  put the snow in the gullies and on the Stuart Glacier bench, where the photo has it
  (`stages/dbg_cover_stuart_s_july.png`).
- **Lidar pits.** The routing kept everything that reached a closed pit: one lidar pit held 14 km
  of snow. Pits now fill to 15 m and deposits are capped at 14 m (real avalanche cones: 5-15 m).
- **Glaciers keep their firn.** Routing shed snow from 30-deg glacier surfaces too, so all ice read
  as grey. On mapped glacier below 35 deg the winter snow is kept (the glacier *is* retained snow),
  and the firn line then falls near the ELA, white above, grey below.
- **Melt is half temperature.** Pure insolation melt kept deep avalanche cones in shaded valley
  bottoms through August (white patches in the Glacier Peak forest). Real low snow melts from warm
  air regardless of aspect. And forest canopy hides ground snow from far away.

Photo check: Rainier NE (July), model snow vs photo snow (L\* threshold) inside the terrain above
1,500 m: IoU 0.68-0.72 across z_sl 1,500-2,500 (`src/calib.py`), best ~1,900-2,100.

### 2.3 The painter (`src/paint.py`, `src/render.py`)

Everything is drawn into an index canvas (material, family, step, haze band); the palette is the
light (as in every good study here), so one canvas re-lights per hour and haze. Stages:

1. **Coverage at 3x supersampling, then a decision per pixel.** Snow cover is smoothed along the
   screen-space *fall line* (1.5 px line-integral) before the threshold, so snow patches stretch
   down the slope as tongues and ribs stretch as arrowheads, never as round blobs or noise.
   Then: majority clean; islands under 3 px absorbed unless the curvature says they are a gully
   (snow) or a rib (rock); one-pixel-thick dashes removed unless they are long ledges.
2. **Materials:** sky, snow, ice (bare glacier), debris-covered ice (below 1,850 m), rock (per-peak
   albedo: weathered granite, andesite, pale dacite), scree (bare + gentle), meadow, forest.
3. **Family from the sub-pixel shade fraction**, not from averaged n.l: a pixel is shade if more
   than half its supersamples face away or sit in cast shadow. Averaging normals first erased every
   rib narrower than a pixel.
4. **Step inside the family** blends an absolute light coordinate (n.l for lit, sky view for shade)
   with a **rank-keyed** one (quantile shares per material: rock lit 30/40/30%, snow lit
   8/22/70%): the ground student's keying and the rock student's value economy. It is what makes
   Stuart's face read at high noon, where true n.l is nearly flat. A **local relief** term
   (n.l minus its neighbourhood mean: Imhof's local adjustment of light) and the curvature steps
   (gullies down, sun-facing ribs up) put the structure in.
5. **Haze bands** from the altitude-weighted haze path, quantized finely (11 edges, 0.6-45 km) and
   3x3-majority cleaned. Two dead ends first: dithered seams made smoke (the aerial student's trap,
   again); coarse bands with big value jumps made blobs across the valley. Beyond 14 / 35 km of
   haze path, each family collapses to 2 / 1 steps.
6. **Colour** = albedo x (sun n.l + sky x sky-view + snow bounce into shade) at each step's
   representative n.l, then Koschmieder mixing to the air colour. Snow's lit ramp is compressed
   (it is a plateau in every photo), rock's is exaggerated. Sky: measured zenith/horizon.
7. **Marks:** transverse crevasses on glacier ice at 11-38 deg (45 m spacing, 6 m open, warped
   into arcs; drawn only when a crack fills a third of a pixel, so they fade into texture with
   distance), with a lit lip above each; cornice lips on lee crests (top step) with the curl's
   shadow under them.

### 2.4 Photo | mine (the honest pairs)

(Photo on the left, box-downsampled to the same pixels, as the answer key.)

**Glacier Peak from the east, 12 km, Aug 30** (`stages/r_gp_e_aug_sept_a6.png`). The best pair.
Silhouette, the summit horns, the white upper glaciers over grey lower ice, the pale dacite
aprons and the dark forested ridge running up the middle are where the photo has them. Wrong: my
sky is duller, the valley forest is darker and flatter than the photo's hazy teal, lit snow is
a step too cold, and the summit rock is too dark and too blocky.

**Rainier from Silver Queen Peak, 21 km, July noon** (`stages/r_rainier_ne_july_summer_v16.png`).
Silhouette exact; Little Tahoma, the Emmons/Winthrop firn, Steamboat Prow and the dark Willis-side
rock masses in place. Wrong: the photo's lower mountain is a warm mid-value band of rock and moraine
that mine renders as scattered grey; a few snow patches still sit in the upper forest; the snow
surface lacks the flow-parallel crevasse streaks of the photo.

**Mount Stuart from the south, 5.6 km, July 2008** (`stages/r_stuart_s_july_summer_g6_crop.png`).
Form right (lidar at 4 m); snow in the right couloirs, including the big central one. Wrong: the
photo's rock is warm gold-grey with sharp dark structural lines (Imhof's rock drawing); mine is a
grey value texture without direction. The gully snow is too wide and too bright at the bottom.

**Far pairs (94 and 96 km)**: the numbers in 1.3 are in the palette logic; the painted 80 km
sheet rows follow them (peak a little lighter than sky, foothills darkest). A painted pair against
the Seattle photo is on the open list.

### 2.5 Sheets

- `sheets/A_<peak>_distance_season_light.png`: rows 5 / 20 / 80 km x September minimum / late May;
  columns clear noon, clear evening, hazy afternoon (visual range 18 km), alpenglow. Peaks: Stuart
  (SSW, Longs Pass side), Rainier (NW, Seattle side), Olympus (NE, Hurricane Ridge side), and the
  Dragontail-Colchuck ridge as the local ridge (N, Colchuck Lake side). The standpoint is on the
  real bearing, on high ground from which the summit is visible and the foreground falls away.
- `sheets/B_vocabulary_scale_season.png`: gully fill, ledge bands, cornice, glacier edge on
  synthetic terrain at 1 and 0.25 m/px, through the same model and painter.

### 2.6 Parameters (the knobs a lane will touch)

`snowmodel.DEFAULT`: `g_acc, z_rs, hs_max` (accumulation), `k_wind, sx_dmax` (wind), `shd_c, shd_a`
(holding depth), `k_gully, gully_r, gully_depth` (couloirs), `pit_cap, hs_cap`, `k_melt, p_ins,
w_temp` (melt), `ice_max_slope`. `SEASONS[...]["z_sl"]` is the one seasonal knob.

`paint.DEFAULT`: `snow_thr, fall_len, min_island, ledge_min_len` (shape cleaning), `key_q, shares,
relief_k, subpix_k, lit_lo, lit_span, seam_w` (values), `bands_km, collapse_km` (air),
`debris_z, crevasses, crev_space, crev_open, crev_slope, cornices, canopy_hide`.
`paint.light_state(sun_el, sun_az, haze_km, warm, airlight, sky_zenith, sky_horizon)`;
`render.add_haze_path(G, cam_z, Ha)` (Ha = aerosol scale height).

For a lane porting this: the snow model is plain numpy + two numba loops and works on any
heightfield (voxsim's worldgen DEM included); the painter wants a G-buffer (normal, distance,
height, sun visibility, material fractions) at 2-3x supersampling.

Bash: Pad synthetic terrain and rebuild vocab
cd ~/work/pixelart-studies/elements/snowpeak/src; python3 - <<'EOF'
s=open("vocab.py").read()
s=s.replace('''    Z = Z - Z.min() + 1800
    return dict(Z=Z.astype(np.float32), ice=ice, trees=np.zeros_like(ice), bare=np.ones_like(ice) * 0.5,
                x0=0.0, y0=float(n * res), res=res)''','''    Z = Z - Z.min() + 1800
    # pad by 1.3 patch widths (edge values) so the camera stands inside the grid (the caster
    # stops at the grid edge)
    p = int(1.3 * n)
    Zp = np.pad(Z, p, mode="edge"); icep = np.pad(ice, p)
    return dict(Z=Zp.astype(np.float32), ice=icep, trees=np.zeros_like(icep), bare=np.ones_like(icep) * 0.5,
                x0=-p * res, y0=float((n + p) * res), res=res, core=(p, n))''')
s=s.replace('''    n = T["Z"].shape[0]; size = n * T["res"]
    tx, ty = size / 2, T["y0"] - size / 2                   # target: centre of the patch
    tz = float(T["Z"][n // 2, n // 2])''','''    p, n = T["core"]; size = n * T["res"]
    tx, ty = size / 2, T["y0"] - (p + n / 2) * T["res"]    # target: centre of the patch
    tz = float(T["Z"][p + n // 2, p + n // 2])''')
open("vocab.py","w").write(s)
EOF
